Flowforge Specification

A language-agnostic design for Flowforge, a durable workflow engine with a typed DSL, a bytecode VM and agent steps, and Sentinel, the scope-enforced asset graph it feeds. The graph model follows the OWASP Amass Open Asset Model (OAM).

Version 0.1 (draft)Date 2026-11-12Audience: implementers in any language (reference targets: Go, then Rust)

1. About this document

1.1 How to use it for code generation

Every section is written to be implementable without reading the others twice. Generate in this order, because each layer depends on the previous one:

  1. Section 11 protobuf contracts, then generate client and server stubs.
  2. Section 10 SQL schema and migrations.
  3. Sections 4, 5, 6 domain types: scope, OAM entities, observations.
  4. Sections 7 and 8 DSL front end, then bytecode and VM.
  5. Sections 9, 12, 13, 14, 15 engine, REST gateway, auth, limiters, notifier.
  6. Sections 24 and 25 first, as a workflow rule: build the workspace, ff-testkit and xtask, then implement one module at a time in the order of 24.7, writing each module's tests, conformance data and benchmarks together with it.
  7. Sections 21, 22, 23 CSR snapshots and analytics, similarity search, distributed-systems rules (the EventStore trait in 23.4 is introduced earlier, at the engine step, with the Postgres implementation).
  8. Section 19 conformance tests, which make a second implementation (Rust) checkable against the first (Go).

1.2 Conventions

TopicRule
Normative wordsMUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD and MAY are used in their RFC 2119 sense.
IdentifiersUUIDv7 in canonical lowercase text form, generated by the service that owns the row. Public IDs carry a prefix in REST: run_, prg_, ent_, apr_, usr_, ten_, wf_.
TimeRFC 3339 in UTC with millisecond precision in JSON (2026-11-12T13:25:11.042Z). Postgres timestamptz. Durations in JSON are integer milliseconds with an _ms suffix.
JSONsnake_case keys, no unknown-field tolerance on requests (reject), full tolerance on responses (ignore).
EnumsLower snake case strings in JSON and SQL; upper snake case in protobuf with an _UNSPECIFIED = 0 value.
TenancyEvery row, message and cache key carries tenant_id. A handler that reads data without filtering by tenant is a defect.
MoneyInteger minor units plus ISO 4217 currency code. Never floats.
ErrorsSee sections 11.7 (gRPC) and 12.2 (REST). Error reason codes are stable identifiers.

1.3 Provenance of the graph model

Section 5 follows the Open Asset Model documentation (owasp-amass.github.io and the asset-db docs). Items I confirmed against those docs are tagged docs. Items that come from the OAM type list or naming but whose exact field names the docs did not give me are tagged verify: check them against the owasp-amass/open-asset-model repository before freezing the schema. Flowforge additions are tagged ff.

2. System overview

2.1 Purpose

Flowforge runs workflows written in a typed DSL. A workflow orchestrates tools (recon tools, ERP actions, LLM calls) as durable, replayable effects. Sentinel stores what the tools learn as a graph of assets and relations. A program's scope bounds everything: no tool may touch a target that scope does not allow.

2.2 Glossary

TermMeaning
TenantAn account boundary. All data and quotas are per tenant.
ProgramA bounded engagement (for example a bug bounty program) with a versioned scope and rules of engagement.
ScopeVersioned allow and deny rules plus the maximum activity class, rate limits and time windows for a program.
WorkflowA named, versioned DSL program that is type-checked, compiled to bytecode and stored.
RunOne execution of a workflow version against a scope version, recorded as an append-only event log.
EffectAn interaction with the outside world requested by the VM (tool call, approval wait, sleep, LLM call, scope check). Effects suspend the VM.
ToolA registered adapter with a typed signature, an effect class and a rate limit.
ObservationA normalized batch of entities, edges and tags emitted by one tool invocation.
Entity / Edge / TagGraph node, directed relation and attached property, as in the Open Asset Model.
PlanDSL source proposed by an agent step. It is checked exactly like human-written code before it can run.
Activity classpassive (public data sources), active (direct traffic to targets), intrusive (testing that can affect availability or data).

2.3 Goals and non-goals

GoalsDurable runs that survive crashes and replay deterministically. A typed DSL where scope and effects are visible to the compiler. A graph compatible with OAM. Per-tenant isolation. Human approval for risky actions. Full audit of every decision.
Non-goals (v0.1)General-purpose programming. Running untrusted native code. Exploit execution. Multi-region active-active. A hosted marketplace of tools.

2.4 Invariants

These MUST hold in every implementation.
  1. Scope: no active or intrusive tool call executes unless the scope service allowed that exact normalized target under the run's scope version, and the decision is in the event log.
  2. Determinism: given the same bytecode and the same recorded effect results, the VM reaches the same state. The VM has no clock, randomness or I/O of its own.
  3. Append-only log: run events are never updated or deleted during a run's life. State is derived from them.
  4. Agents propose, the engine disposes: an agent can only emit DSL source. Type, effect, scope and policy checks run outside the model.
  5. Tenant isolation: no query, cache key or event crosses a tenant boundary.
  6. No silent success: an unreachable scope service, rate limiter or policy engine means deny, not allow.

3. Architecture

3.1 Services

ServiceOwnsSync APIAsync
gatewayUsers, tenants, sessions, API keys (identity module)REST /v1 (public), gRPC client to allPublishes auth.events
engineWorkflows, runs, run events, effects, approvals, timersEngineServicePublishes run.events, approval.events
tool-runnerTool registry (code), sandboxes, per-tool rate stateToolRunnerServiceNone
scopePrograms, scope versions, decision logScopeServicePublishes scope.events
graphEntities, edges, tags, observations, change events, search indexGraphServicePublishes graph.changes (outbox)
similarityEmbeddings index (pgvector, then DiskANN segments), tombstones, hybrid searchSimilarity (section 22.9)Subscribes to graph.changes
notifierAlert rules, deliveries, dedupe stateNotifierService (admin)Subscribes to all event topics

3.2 Communication rules

  • gRPC (HTTP/2, TLS) for request/response and streaming between services. Every call carries metadata authorization (internal token), x-tenant-id, x-request-id, traceparent, and an optional idempotency-key. Every call sets a deadline.
  • Pub/Sub for fan-out events. Producers write events to an outbox table in the same transaction as the state change; a relay publishes them at-least-once. Consumers MUST be idempotent, keyed by event_id.
  • Only gateway is reachable from the internet. Internal services trust the gateway's internal token, never a client-supplied identity.
  • tool-runner has no direct internet access. Its only route out is the scope-checked egress proxy.

3.3 Life of a run

  1. Client calls POST /v1/runs with a workflow version, inputs and Idempotency-Key. The gateway authenticates, authorizes, and forwards SubmitRun.
  2. Engine pins the current scope version, loads bytecode, records run_submitted, and schedules the run.
  3. A scheduler worker leases the run, loads recorded events, and drives the VM until it suspends on an effect.
  4. For a tool effect the engine calls scope.Check, records scope_decision, then ToolRunner.Execute. The runner re-checks scope per target and streams observations.
  5. Engine records effect_completed and forwards observations to graph.IngestObservations. Graph writes entities and edges, computes changes, and emits graph.changes.
  6. Notifier evaluates alert rules on those changes. Agent and approval effects follow the same record-then-act pattern.
  7. When the VM returns, the engine records run_succeeded or run_failed, and the final state is derived from the log.

4. Scope model

4.1 Program scope document

{
  "program_id": "prg_01JD8K2XH3",
  "version": 7,
  "name": "Acme public bug bounty",
  "effective_from": "2026-11-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "rules": [
    { "id": "r1", "effect": "allow", "kind": "domain",          "value": "acme.com",          "note": "apex only" },
    { "id": "r2", "effect": "allow", "kind": "wildcard_domain", "value": "*.acme.com",        "note": "all subdomains, any depth" },
    { "id": "r3", "effect": "allow", "kind": "cidr",            "value": "203.0.113.0/24" },
    { "id": "r4", "effect": "deny",  "kind": "domain",          "value": "payments.acme.com", "note": "explicitly excluded" },
    { "id": "r5", "effect": "deny",  "kind": "wildcard_domain", "value": "*.corp.acme.com" },
    { "id": "r6", "effect": "allow", "kind": "url_prefix",      "value": "https://api.acme.com/v2/" }
  ],
  "activity": {
    "max_class": "active",
    "prohibited": ["dos", "social_engineering", "credential_stuffing"],
    "rate": { "requests_per_second": 5, "burst": 10, "concurrency": 4 },
    "windows": [ { "days": ["mon","tue","wed","thu","fri"], "start": "09:00", "end": "18:00", "tz": "UTC" } ]
  }
}
FieldRules
rules[].kinddomain (exact name), wildcard_domain (*. prefix; matches any depth below the name, never the apex itself), cidr (IPv4 or IPv6 prefix), url_prefix (scheme, host, port and path prefix on a segment boundary), asn (value AS64500, allowed only as informational input to discovery, never as sole permission for active work).
rules[].effectallow or deny. Deny always wins over allow. No match means deny.
activity.max_classHighest activity class any run of this program may perform. A workflow whose inferred effect exceeds this fails the compile-time policy check and is also refused at run time.
activity.rateProgram-level ceiling, enforced by the limiter (section 14) in addition to tenant and tool limits.
activity.windowsAllowed testing windows. Outside a window only passive effects may run; active ones wait.
versionMonotonic integer. A run is pinned to one version. Publishing a new version pauses in-flight runs of that program at their next effect boundary and re-validates them.

4.2 Normalization (MUST run before any match)

  1. Domain names: trim, lowercase, remove one trailing dot, convert to IDNA A-label (punycode), reject empty labels, reject names longer than 253 octets or labels longer than 63.
  2. URLs: parse; lowercase scheme and host; strip default ports (:80, :443); drop fragment; reject userinfo (credentials in URL) for scope decisions; percent-decode unreserved characters; collapse . and .. path segments.
  3. IP addresses: parse strictly (no octal, hex or decimal-integer IPv4 forms); output canonical text; map IPv4-mapped IPv6 to IPv4.
  4. Anything that fails to normalize yields decision deny with reason invalid_target.

4.3 Decision algorithm

function check(target, class, scope, now) -> Decision
  t = normalize(target)                       // section 4.2
  if t is invalid:                            return deny("invalid_target")
  if class > scope.activity.max_class:        return deny("class_exceeds_program")
  if class != passive and not in_window(now): return deny("outside_window")
  for rule in scope.rules where rule.effect == deny:
      if matches(rule, t): return deny("explicit_deny", rule.id)
  if class != passive and t is hostname:
      ips = resolve_once(t)                   // pinned for the connection that follows
      for ip in ips:
          for rule in scope.rules where rule.effect == deny and rule.kind == cidr:
              if matches(rule, ip): return deny("resolved_ip_denied", rule.id)
  for rule in scope.rules where rule.effect == allow:
      if matches(rule, t): return allow(rule.id, pinned_ips=ips)
  return deny("no_matching_allow")

A decision is { decision, reason, rule_id, scope_version, target, pinned_ips[], decided_at }. The same object is stored in the scope_decisions table and the run's event log.

4.4 Enforcement layers

LayerMechanismFailure mode
Compile timeActive tools accept only InScope<T> parameters. The only constructor is scope.check(x).Program does not compile (error E0301).
EngineCalls scope.Check before scheduling a tool effect and records the decision.Deny: effect fails with scope_denied.
Tool-runnerRe-checks per concrete target before starting a process (tools may expand one input into many targets).Deny: target skipped and logged.
Egress proxyEvery outbound connection is checked against the pinned decision: host, resolved IP, redirects.Connection refused and logged.
Graph ingestEvery entity gets scope_status (in_scope, out_of_scope, unknown). Only in_scope entities may become inputs to active steps.Entity stored for context but never targeted.
AgentPlans are compiled and checked like any source.Plan rejected, counted as a metric.
Kill switchPer program and global flag checked by the engine and the proxy.Runs cancelled, new calls blocked.

4.5 Edge cases the implementation MUST handle

  • Redirects and CNAME chains: re-run check on every hop. A redirect to an out-of-scope host is not followed.
  • Check-then-use race: connect only to pinned_ips from the allow decision. Never re-resolve between check and connect.
  • Shared hosting and CDNs: permission attaches to the hostname rule, never to an IP alone.
  • URL tricks: https://acme.com@evil.com/, https://acme.com.evil.com/, mixed case, trailing dot, punycode lookalikes, IPv6 zones, decimal IPs. All covered by normalization plus host-only matching.
  • Scope changes mid-run: see version semantics above.

5. Graph model (Open Asset Model aligned)

5.1 Principles

  • The graph stores entities (nodes holding one OAM asset), edges (typed, directed relations between entities) and tags (properties attached to an entity or an edge). This mirrors the OAM and its asset-db library, where each of these also carries created_at and last_seen.
  • Asset and relation type names, labels and field names use OAM names so that data can be exported to or imported from Amass-compatible tooling.
  • Flowforge adds tenant, program, scope status, confidence, provenance and lifecycle columns around the OAM content (tagged ff). They never change the meaning of the OAM content.
  • OAM assets are JSON documents. The graph keeps them as a jsonb column plus a canonical key used for de-duplication.

5.2 Taxonomy

CategoryAsset types
Network infrastructureFQDN, IPAddress, Netblock, AutonomousSystem
RegistrationDomainRecord, IPNetRecord, AutnumRecord
Digital artifactsURL, Service, TLSCertificate, File
Organization and peopleOrganization, Person, Location, Phone, ContactRecord
IdentityIdentifier
FinancialAccount, FundsTransfer
ProductProduct, ProductRelease

The OAM docs list these 21 types and describe the list as open-ended. Flowforge v0.1 implements the network, registration, digital-artifact and product groups, and stores unrecognized or later types opaquely (type name plus JSON content) so imports do not fail.

5.3 Core structures

// Entity
{
  "id": "ent_01JD8K3A7Q",
  "tenant_id": "ten_01JD...", "program_id": "prg_01JD8K2XH3",      // ff
  "asset": { "type": "FQDN", "name": "api.acme.com" },             // OAM asset (content)
  "key": "fqdn:api.acme.com",                                      // ff canonical dedup key
  "created_at": "2026-11-12T13:25:11.042Z",                        // OAM
  "last_seen":  "2026-11-12T14:02:12.311Z",                        // OAM
  "scope_status": "in_scope",                                      // ff: in_scope | out_of_scope | unknown
  "confidence": 0.93,                                              // ff: 0..1
  "state": "active",                                               // ff: active | stale
  "first_seen_run": "run_7f3a91", "last_seen_run": "run_7f3a91"    // ff
}

// Edge
{
  "id": "edg_01JD8K3B1Z",
  "relation": { "type": "BasicDNSRelation", "label": "dns_record",
                "header": { "rr_type": 1, "class": 1, "ttl": 300 } },
  "from_entity": "ent_01JD8K3A7Q", "to_entity": "ent_01JD8K3A9W",
  "created_at": "...", "last_seen": "...",
  "confidence": 0.97, "tenant_id": "ten_01JD...", "program_id": "prg_01JD8K2XH3"   // ff
}

// Tag (EntityTag or EdgeTag)
{
  "id": "tag_01JD8K3C5M",
  "target": { "kind": "entity", "id": "ent_01JD8K3A7Q" },
  "property": { "type": "SourceProperty", "name": "subfinder", "value": "confidence=80" },
  "created_at": "...", "last_seen": "..."
}

5.4 Asset types

The canonical key is the Flowforge de-duplication key (ff), unique per (tenant, program, type, key). OAM docs did not specify a dedup key for most types, so these are Flowforge decisions. Status tags show how I sourced each field list.

AssetContent fieldsCanonical keySource
FQDNnamefqdn:<normalized name> (section 4.2)verify fields
IPAddressaddress, ip_version (IPv4 | IPv6)ip:<canonical text>docs address; verify ip_version (OAM's Go struct names this field Type; renamed here to avoid clashing with the asset discriminator)
Netblockcidr, ip_versionnet:<cidr, host bits zeroed>verify
AutonomousSystemnumberasn:<number>verify
URLurl, scheme, username, password, host, port (number), path, options, fragmenturl:<normalized url without fragment>docs
Serviceunique_id, service_type, output, output_length (int), attributes (object)svc:<unique_id>, where unique_id = sha256(parent_key + "/" + port + "/" + protocol + "/" + service_type)docs fields
TLSCertificateversion, serial_number, subject_common_name, issuer_common_name, not_before, not_after, key_usage[], signature_algorithm, public_key_algorithm, is_ca, crl_distribution_points[], subject_key_id, authority_key_idtls:<sha256 of DER>verify
Fileurl, name, typefile:<sha256 of url>verify
DomainRecordraw, id, domain, punycode, name, extension, whois_server, created_date, updated_date, expiration_date, status[], dnssecdomrec:<domain>verify
IPNetRecordraw, cidr, handle, start_address, end_address, type, name, method, country, parent_handle, whois_server, created_date, updated_date, status[]ipnet:<handle or cidr>verify
AutnumRecordraw, number, handle, name, whois_server, created_date, updated_date, status[]autnum:<number>verify
Organizationunique_id, name, legal_name, founding_date, jurisdiction, registration_id, industry, target_markets[], active, non_profit, headcountorg:<unique_id>docs
Person, Location, Phone, ContactRecord, Identifier, Account, FundsTransfer, Product, ProductReleaseStored as opaque JSON in v0.1 (type name preserved; ContactRecord includes discovered_at per the docs example)<type>:<sha256 of canonical JSON>verify before modeling

Common to every asset per the docs: type, created_at, last_seen. The docs also mention id, confidence and source as shared metadata; Flowforge keeps those as entity columns and tags rather than inside the asset content.

5.5 Relations

OAM defines five relation types, which determine the JSON shape, and many relation labels, which determine the meaning.

Relation typeFieldsUsed for
SimpleRelationlabelGeneric non-DNS links. docs
PortRelationlabel, port_number, protocolA host or URL bound to a service port. docs
BasicDNSRelationlabel, header { rr_type, class, ttl }A, AAAA, CNAME, NS records. verify header field names
PrefDNSRelationas Basic plus preferenceMX records. verify
SRVDNSRelationas Basic plus priority, weight, portSRV records. verify

Every relation also carries a source (discovery) property and timestamps. Direction is meaningful: from_entity → to_entity reads as "from has label to".

LabelRelation typeFrom → ToMeaningSource
dns_recordBasic / Pref / SRV DNSFQDN → IPAddress or FQDNResolved A/AAAA, or CNAME/NS/MX/SRV targetdocs

The docs call the label list partial and extensible. The graph service MUST accept unknown labels on a known relation type and MUST validate only the endpoint types listed above for labels marked docs.

5.6 Properties (tags)

Property typeShapeUse
SourcePropertyname (data source or plugin), confidence (int 0-100)Provenance of an entity or edge. One per source. docs
SimplePropertyname, value (strings; Flowforge’s names, OAM upstream uses property_name, property_value)Generic key-value, for example last_monitored. docs
DNSRecordPropertyproperty_name, header, dataDNS record data that is not a graph edge (TXT, SOA). docs (type named), verify fields
VulnPropertyid, desc, source, category, enum, refVulnerability reference attached to an entity. docs (type named), verify fields
FlowforgePropertyname, value (JSON)Agent notes, triage rank, hypotheses. Always carries kind: fact or hypothesis. ff

5.7 Ingest and merge semantics

  1. Resolve: for each entity in an observation compute key. Upsert by (tenant, program, asset type, key). If new, set created_at = last_seen = observed_at. If existing, set last_seen = max(last_seen, observed_at) and merge content with non-empty wins, newest wins on conflict.
  2. Edges: upsert by (from_entity, to_entity, relation type, label, relation discriminator), where the discriminator is port_number+protocol for port relations and rr_type for DNS relations.
  3. Provenance: add one SourceProperty per (tool, entity or edge). Confidence from several independent sources combines as 1 - Π(1 - cᵢ), where each cᵢ is the highest confidence reported by one source, and is capped at 0.99.
  4. Scope status: computed by calling the same matcher as section 4 in passive mode (no resolution). Unknown is never promoted to in-scope by inference.
  5. Staleness: an entity becomes stale when the sources that previously reported it completed a covering run (same tool, same input) and did not report it for stale_after_runs (default 3) consecutive runs. It becomes active again when seen. Nothing is hard-deleted; deletion is an explicit admin action recorded in the audit log.
  6. Change events are produced in the same transaction through the outbox, one per effective change: entity_added, entity_changed (with a JSON patch), entity_stale, entity_reactivated, edge_added, edge_removed (stale), scope_status_changed.

5.8 Example fragment

{
  "entities": [
    { "ref": "a", "asset": { "type": "FQDN", "name": "api.acme.com" } },
    { "ref": "b", "asset": { "type": "IPAddress", "address": "203.0.113.10", "ip_version": "IPv4" } },
    { "ref": "c", "asset": { "type": "Service", "unique_id": "5c1e...", "service_type": "http",
                              "output": "HTTP/1.1 200 OK ...", "output_length": 412,
                              "attributes": { "server": ["nginx/1.25.3"] } } },
    { "ref": "d", "asset": { "type": "Product", "name": "nginx" } }
  ],
  "edges": [
    { "from": "a", "to": "b", "relation": { "type": "BasicDNSRelation", "label": "dns_record", "header": { "rr_type": 1, "class": 1, "ttl": 300 } } },
    { "from": "b", "to": "c", "relation": { "type": "PortRelation", "label": "port", "port_number": 443, "protocol": "tcp" } },
    { "from": "c", "to": "d", "relation": { "type": "SimpleRelation", "label": "product_used" } }
  ]
}

In JSON the asset discriminator is the type key inside asset. Storage splits it: asset_type is a column, and the remaining fields are the content column (section 10).

5.9 Traversal and analysis operations

OperationSemantics
neighbors(entity, labels?, direction, depth, since?)Breadth-first, bounded depth (max 6). Returns entities and edges, each at most once. since filters on last_seen.
path(from, to, max_cost)Dijkstra over edges with cost -ln(confidence), minimizing total cost. Returns the path and its combined confidence.
blast_radius(entity, depth)Entities reachable through ownership and hosting labels (dns_record, port, certificate, contains, owns), ranked by distance.
diff(run_a, run_b) or diff(since)Set difference of entity and edge keys between two points; changed content reported as JSON patches. Implemented from change events, not by scanning.
components()Connected components via union-find, used to group assets into services or organizations.
search(query, types?, limit)Trigram index over entity display text (names, addresses, URLs, banners). Substring and typo tolerant; results verified by exact substring check, then ranked by trigram similarity.

6. Observations and tool adapters

6.1 Observation envelope

Tools never write to the graph. They return observations, and the engine forwards them to GraphService.IngestObservations.

{
  "observation_id": "obs_01JD8K3M2Q",
  "tenant_id": "ten_01JD...", "program_id": "prg_01JD8K2XH3", "run_id": "run_7f3a91",
  "step": "probe", "effect_seq": 14,
  "tool": { "name": "httpx", "version": "1.6.9" },
  "observed_at": "2026-11-12T14:02:12.311Z",
  "confidence": 0.9,
  "entities": [ { "ref": "u1", "asset": { "type": "URL", "url": "https://api.acme.com/", "scheme": "https", "host": "api.acme.com", "port": 443 } } ],
  "edges":    [ { "from": "u1", "to": "ent_01JD8K3A7Q", "relation": { "type": "SimpleRelation", "label": "domain" } } ],
  "tags":     [ { "target": "u1", "property": { "type": "SimpleProperty", "name": "title", "value": "Acme API" } } ],
  "raw_ref": "gs://flowforge-artifacts-prod/ten_01JD.../obs_01JD8K3M2Q.json.zst"
}
  • ref values are local to the envelope. An edge endpoint is either a local ref or an existing entity id.
  • Raw tool output is stored in object storage and referenced by raw_ref. It is never fed to an agent directly (section 16).
  • An envelope MUST be idempotent: ingesting the same observation_id twice has no additional effect.

6.2 Tool specification

{
  "name": "httpx",
  "version": "1.6.9",
  "description": "HTTP probing and fingerprinting",
  "input":  { "type": "InScope<Host>" },
  "output": { "type": "Stream<HttpService>", "observes": ["URL", "Service", "Product", "TLSCertificate"] },
  "effect_class": "active",
  "rate": { "requests_per_second": 20, "burst": 40, "concurrency": 8 },
  "timeout_ms": 300000,
  "retry": { "max_attempts": 3, "retry_on": ["unavailable", "deadline_exceeded"], "backoff": { "initial_ms": 1000, "max_ms": 30000, "multiplier": 2.0, "jitter": "full" } },
  "idempotent": true,
  "sandbox": { "image": "registry/tools/httpx@sha256:...", "cpu_limit": "1", "memory_limit_mb": 512, "network": "egress_proxy_only", "read_only_fs": true }
}

6.3 Adapter contract

  1. An adapter implements: spec() → ToolSpec, and run(input, ctx) → stream of Observation.
  2. The adapter MUST NOT open network connections itself except through the proxy provided in ctx.
  3. It MUST stream results as they are produced (batches of at most 500 entities), and MUST stop within 2 seconds of a cancel signal.
  4. It MUST normalize values with the functions in section 4.2 before emitting entities, so keys match across tools.
  5. It reports progress as { items_done, items_total? } and structured errors with a retryable flag.

6.4 Example tool mappings

Tool (example)ClassEmits
Subdomain enumeration (passive sources)passiveFQDN entities; SourceProperty per source
DNS resolutionpassive (public resolvers) or active (direct to authoritative)IPAddress; dns_record edges with DNS headers; CNAME edges between FQDNs
ASN and netblock lookuppassiveAutonomousSystem, Netblock; announces, contains edges
Certificate transparency querypassiveTLSCertificate; common_name, san_dns_name edges to FQDN
HTTP probingactiveURL, Service, Product; domain, ip_address, port, product_used edges
Port scanning (rate-limited)activeService entities; port edges with port_number and protocol
TLS handshakeactiveTLSCertificate; certificate edge from Service
WHOIS and RDAPpassiveDomainRecord, IPNetRecord, AutnumRecord, Organization; registration edges

Class assignments are defaults; a program can only tighten them.

7. The Flowforge language (DSL)

The DSL is a small, statically typed, expression-oriented language. It has immutable bindings, algebraic data types, pattern matching, closures, and effects that are tracked by the type checker. Source files use the extension .ff, are UTF-8, and have no preprocessor.

7.1 Example programs

// Declarations of registered tools are generated from the tool registry (section 6.2).
tool subfinder(domain: Fqdn)        -> Stream<Fqdn>         ! passive
tool resolve(name: Fqdn)            -> Option<IpAddress>    ! passive
tool httpx(host: InScope<Host>)     -> Option<HttpService> ! active

type HttpService = { url: Url, status: Int, title: Option<String>, tech: List<Product> }
type Report      = { new_assets: Int, alerts: Int }

#[timeout(2h)]
workflow recon(roots: List<Fqdn>) -> Report {
  let subs: Stream<Fqdn> = roots |> flat_map(subfinder) |> dedupe;
  let live = subs |> filter_map(resolve);                          // Stream<IpAddress>

  #[retry(max = 3, backoff = exp(1s, 30s))]
  let web = subs
    |> filter(n => scope.allows(n))                               // cheap passive filter
    |> par_map(n => httpx(scope.check(Host::Name(n))?), limit = 8)
    |> filter_map(x => x);

  for svc in web {
    match svc.tech |> first(p => p.name == "jenkins") {
      Some(p) => emit_alert(Severity::High, "Jenkins exposed: {svc.url}"),
      None    => (),
    }
  }
  Report { new_assets: len(web), alerts: 0 }
}
// ERP-style workflow with approval and compensation (saga)
tool extract_invoice(doc: DocRef) -> Invoice                       ! read
tool post_entry(e: LedgerEntry)    -> PostingId                    ! financial_write
tool reverse_entry(id: PostingId)  -> Unit                         ! financial_write

workflow post_invoice(doc: DocRef) -> Result<PostingId, String> {
  let inv = extract_invoice(doc);
  require approval(role = "finance_ap", reason = "post {inv.total} to ledger", timeout = 24h);
  let id = post_entry(entry_for(inv)) compensate reverse_entry(it);
  Ok(id)
}
// Agent step: the model proposes a plan as DSL source; the engine checks it before anything runs.
workflow triage(ctx: GraphSummary) -> Report {
  let plan: Plan<Report> = agent planner(
      goal    = "Probe newly discovered hosts that look like admin or CI systems",
      context = ctx,
      tools   = [resolve, httpx],
      budget  = Budget { tokens = 20000, tool_calls = 25, iterations = 3 });
  let result = execute(plan)?;        // plan was type-, effect- and scope-checked when produced
  result
}

7.2 Lexical grammar

TokenDefinition
WhitespaceSpace, tab, newline, carriage return. Not significant except to separate tokens.
Comments// to end of line and nestable /* block */.
Identifier[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*. Types, enum variants and records start with an uppercase letter by convention and by the checker's naming rule W0101.
Keywordsworkflow fn tool type enum let for in match if else return require approval agent compensate true false as use
Integer[0-9][0-9_]* and 0x[0-9a-fA-F_]+. 64-bit signed. Overflow is a compile error for literals and a run-time failure arith_overflow for operations.
Float[0-9][0-9_]*\.[0-9][0-9_]*([eE][+-]?[0-9]+)?. IEEE 754 binary64. NaN is not producible by literals.
DurationInteger followed by ms s m h d, for example 250ms, 2h. Stored as signed 64-bit nanoseconds.
String"..." with escapes \n \t \" \\ \u{HEX}. {expr} inside a string interpolates the expression (it must have type String, Int, Float, Bool, Duration, Time, or a type with a built-in display). Use {{ and }} for literal braces.
Operators+ - * / % == != < <= > >= && || ! |> => -> ? . .. :: = : , ; ( ) [ ] { } # ! (the effect marker ! is the same token as logical not; the parser disambiguates by position).

7.3 Syntactic grammar (EBNF)

Notation: [ x ] optional, { x } zero or more, ( a | b ) choice, quoted text is a token. Expressions use precedence climbing (table below).

program      = { item } ;
item         = [ annotations ] ( workflow_decl | fn_decl | tool_decl | type_decl | enum_decl | use_decl ) ;
annotations  = { "#" "[" annotation { "," annotation } "]" } ;
annotation   = IDENT [ "(" [ arg { "," arg } ] ")" ] ;

use_decl     = "use" path ";" ;
path         = IDENT { "::" IDENT } ;

workflow_decl= "workflow" IDENT "(" [ params ] ")" [ "->" type ] [ effect ] block ;
fn_decl      = "fn" IDENT [ generics ] "(" [ params ] ")" [ "->" type ] [ effect ] block ;
tool_decl    = "tool" IDENT "(" [ params ] ")" "->" type effect ;          (* body-less: bound to registry *)
effect       = "!" IDENT { "," IDENT } ;                                    (* e.g. ! passive, financial_write *)
params       = param { "," param } ;
param        = IDENT ":" type ;
generics     = "<" IDENT { "," IDENT } ">" ;

type_decl    = "type" IDENT [ generics ] "=" "{" [ field { "," field } [ "," ] ] "}" ;
field        = IDENT ":" type ;
enum_decl    = "enum" IDENT [ generics ] "{" variant { "," variant } [ "," ] "}" ;
variant      = IDENT [ "(" type { "," type } ")" ] ;

type         = IDENT [ "<" type { "," type } ">" ]
             | "(" [ type { "," type } ] ")"                  (* tuple or unit *)
             | "(" [ type { "," type } ] ")" "->" type       (* function type *) ;

block        = "{" { stmt } [ expr ] "}" ;
stmt         = [ annotations ] let_stmt
             | for_stmt
             | require_stmt
             | "return" [ expr ] ";"
             | expr ";" ;
let_stmt     = "let" pattern [ ":" type ] "=" expr [ "compensate" expr ] ";" ;
for_stmt     = "for" pattern "in" expr block ;
require_stmt = "require" "approval" "(" [ arg { "," arg } ] ")" ";" ;

expr         = pipe ;
pipe         = or { "|>" or } ;
or           = and { "||" and } ;
and          = equality { "&&" equality } ;
equality     = relational [ ( "==" | "!=" ) relational ] ;
relational   = range [ ( "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=" | "in" ) range ] ;
range        = additive [ ".." additive ] ;
additive     = multiplicative { ( "+" | "-" ) multiplicative } ;
multiplicative = unary { ( "*" | "/" | "%" ) unary } ;
unary        = ( "-" | "!" ) unary | postfix ;
postfix      = primary { "(" [ args ] ")" | "[" expr "]" | "." IDENT | "?" | "as" type } ;
primary      = literal | IDENT | path | "(" [ expr { "," expr } ] ")"
             | "[" [ expr { "," expr } ] "]"
             | record_lit | lambda | if_expr | match_expr | agent_expr | block ;
record_lit   = path "{" [ IDENT ":" expr { "," IDENT ":" expr } ] "}" ;
lambda       = ( IDENT | "(" [ IDENT { "," IDENT } ] ")" ) "=>" expr ;
if_expr      = "if" expr block [ "else" ( if_expr | block ) ] ;
match_expr   = "match" expr "{" arm { "," arm } [ "," ] "}" ;
arm          = pattern [ "if" expr ] "=>" expr ;
agent_expr   = "agent" IDENT "(" [ named_arg { "," named_arg } ] ")" ;
args         = arg { "," arg } ;
arg          = [ IDENT ( "=" | ":" ) ] expr ;
named_arg    = IDENT "=" expr ;

pattern      = "_" | IDENT | literal | path [ "(" pattern { "," pattern } ")" ]
             | "(" pattern { "," pattern } ")" | path "{" IDENT [ ":" pattern ] { "," ... } "}" ;
literal      = INT | FLOAT | STRING | DURATION | "true" | "false" ;
Level (low → high)OperatorsAssociativity
1|>left; a |> f(x) means f(a, x), and a |> f means f(a)
2||left, short-circuit
3&&left, short-circuit
4== !=non-associative
5< <= > >= innon-associative
6..non-associative (half-open integer range)
7+ -left
8* / %left
9unary - !right
10call, index, field, ?, asleft

A lambda x => e extends as far right as possible. A block's final expression without a semicolon is its value; with a semicolon the block's value is ().

7.4 Types

GroupTypes
PrimitiveInt (i64), Float (f64), Bool, String (UTF-8), Duration, Time (Unix nanoseconds), Unit written ()
CompositeList<T>, Map<K, V> (K is String, Int or a domain scalar), Option<T> (Some, None), Result<T, E> (Ok, Err), Stream<T>, tuples, records, enums, function types
Domain (OAM-named)Fqdn, IpAddress, Netblock, Asn, Url, Service, TlsCertificate, Product, Organization, and Host (built-in enum Name(Fqdn) | Ip(IpAddress)). Each is a distinct newtype validated on construction; there is no implicit conversion between them.
ScopeScope (the pinned document), InScope<T> (proof that T passed scope.check), ScopeDenied (error record)
EnginePlan<T>, Budget, Approval, Severity (enum Info | Low | Medium | High | Critical), RunInfo

Stream<T> in v0.1 is a materialized, ordered, chunked sequence produced by an effect. All list operations also work on streams. Laziness is not part of the language semantics.

7.5 Static semantics

  1. Name resolution: lexical scoping, bindings are immutable, shadowing in an inner block is allowed, redefinition in the same block is an error (E0202). Items are mutually visible in any order within a file.
  2. Type checking is bidirectional: literals, constructors and annotated parameters synthesize types; lambdas, empty lists and None are checked against an expected type. Generic functions use rank-1 parametric polymorphism with unification at the call site. No subtyping and no implicit coercions.
  3. Exhaustiveness: a match must cover all variants, or end with a wildcard. A pattern that can never match is a warning (W0501). Guards do not count toward exhaustiveness.
  4. The ? operator applies to Result<T, E> and Option<T>. In a function whose return type is Result<_, E2> the error type must be equal to E2 (no implicit conversion). In a workflow with a non-Result return, ? failing ends the run with status failed and code unwrap_failed.
  5. Effects: every expression has an effect set. A function's inferred effect set is the union of its body's. The declared set after !, when present, MUST be a superset of the inferred set (E0401); a declaration that is too broad is a warning (W0402). Calling a tool contributes the tool's declared effects. Effects are: read, passive, active, intrusive, write, financial_write, llm, time. The activity classes order as passive < active < intrusive.
  6. Scope typing: a tool parameter of type InScope<T> accepts only an expression of that type. The only ways to obtain one are scope.check(x: T) -> Result<InScope<T>, ScopeDenied> (an effect) and passing along a parameter that already has that type. An InScope<T> value cannot be stored in a record type declared outside the standard library, which prevents smuggling proofs across runs (E0302).
  7. Policy check (compile-time): for a program, the inferred effect set of a workflow is compared with the program's max_class; financial_write and intrusive require a reachable require approval or approve(...) before the first effect that uses them (E0403). The checker is told the program's policy; with no program it checks only internal consistency.
  8. Annotations: #[timeout(d)] on workflows and let-statements, #[retry(max = n, backoff = exp(initial, max))] on let-statements whose right side performs effects, #[idempotent] and #[deprecated("msg")] on items. Unknown annotations are errors (E0601).
  9. Compensation: let x = e compensate c; requires that e performs a write or financial_write effect; c is an expression evaluated with it bound to the value of x. Compensations run in reverse order of registration on failure or cancellation (section 9.6).

7.6 Standard library (pure unless marked)

FunctionTypeNotes
map, filter, filter_map, flat_mapList<A> or Stream<A>, (A) -> B ... Closures may perform effects; evaluated sequentially in order.
par_map(Stream<A>, (A) -> B, limit: Int) -> Stream<B>Effect-aware concurrency; result order equals input order. Option on_error = fail | collect.
fold, any, all, first, count, len, take, skip, sort_by, group_by, dedupe, zip, enumerate, reverse, concatusualdedupe uses structural equality. sort_by is stable.
contains, starts_with, ends_with, split, join, trim, lower, upper, replaceString and List helpersUnicode-aware, locale-independent.
parse_int, parse_float, to_stringconversionsParsers return Option.
scope.allows(x)(T) -> BoolLocal passive pre-filter using the pinned document (no effect, not a proof).
similar(e, k), embed(text)(Entity, Int) -> List<Entity>, (String) -> VectorEffects similarity_query and embed (section 22.9). Results are hints: pass them through scope.check before acting on them.
scope.check(x)(T) -> Result<InScope<T>, ScopeDenied>Effect scope_check. Records a decision.
approve(role, reason, timeout)-> Result<Approval, Denied>Effect. require approval(...) is sugar for approve(...)? with failure code approval_denied.
sleep(d), now()Unit, TimeEffects (time); now() is recorded, so replay returns the original value.
emit_alert(sev, msg)UnitEffect; publishes a workflow_alert event.
execute(plan)Plan<T> -> Result<T, PlanError>Effect; runs an accepted plan as a child run.
log(level, msg)UnitRecorded as an event with a size cap.

7.7 Diagnostics format

{
  "code": "E0302",
  "severity": "error",
  "message": "InScope<Host> cannot be stored in a record field",
  "primary":   { "file": "recon.ff", "start": { "line": 14, "col": 22, "offset": 301 }, "end": { "line": 14, "col": 40, "offset": 319 }, "label": "this value is a scope proof" },
  "secondary": [ { "file": "recon.ff", "start": { "line": 3, "col": 1, "offset": 40 }, "end": { "line": 3, "col": 12, "offset": 51 }, "label": "record declared here" } ],
  "help": "Store the Host and call scope.check again where it is used."
}
RangeAreaExamples
E0001-E0099LexingE0001 unterminated string, E0002 invalid escape, E0003 integer too large, E0004 unknown duration unit
E0100-E0199ParsingE0101 unexpected token, E0102 missing semicolon, E0103 non-associative operator chain
E0200-E0299NamesE0201 unknown name, E0202 duplicate binding, E0203 unknown tool
E0300-E0399TypesE0301 argument type mismatch, E0302 scope proof misuse, E0303 arity mismatch, E0304 cannot infer type
E0400-E0499Effects and policyE0401 effect not declared, E0402 effect exceeds program class, E0403 missing approval
E0500-E0599PatternsE0501 non-exhaustive match
E0600-E0699Annotations and limitsE0601 unknown annotation, E0602 program too large
W-codesWarningsW0101 naming, W0402 over-broad effect declaration, W0501 unreachable pattern

The compiler returns all diagnostics it can produce, in source order, after parser error recovery (synchronize at ;, } and item keywords). The same format is used for agent plans and is fed back to the agent (section 16).

8. Bytecode and virtual machine

8.1 Execution model

  • A stack machine with a value stack, a call-frame stack and per-frame locals. Bindings are immutable, so closures capture by value and no upvalue closing is needed.
  • Memory is managed per run: values are reference-counted immutable objects, or allocated in a per-run arena that is freed when the run ends. A tracing GC is not required by this specification.
  • The VM is a pure function of (module, recorded effect results). It never reads clocks, randomness, the environment or the network.
  • The VM runs until it completes, fails, exhausts fuel, or suspends on an effect:
run(state, fuel)  -> Suspended { seq, kind, args } | Completed { value } | Failed { code, message, span } | OutOfFuel
resume(state, effect_result: Ok(value) | Err(error_value)) -> same as run
snapshot(state)   -> bytes   (canonical CBOR, section 8.7)
restore(module, bytes) -> state

8.2 Values

TagPayloadNotes
Unit, Boolnone, 1 bit
Int, Duration, Timei64Checked arithmetic; overflow raises arith_overflow.
Floatf64NaN results raise arith_nan. Equality is IEEE except NaN cannot occur.
StringUTF-8 bytesMax 1 MiB.
List, Streamvector of valuesMax 1,000,000 elements. Stream is a list with a flag for display purposes.
Mapordered mapIteration order is insertion order; keys compared structurally.
Recordtype id + field valuesFields in declaration order.
Enumtype id + variant index + payload valuesOption and Result are enums with fixed type ids.
Closurefunction id + captured values
Domain scalarvalidated canonical string or bytesFqdn, IpAddress, Netblock, Asn, Url. Constructed only by validating builtins or tool results.
ScopeProoftype tag + canonical target + decision idRuntime form of InScope<T>; not serializable into outputs.
EntityRefentity idReference to a graph entity, resolved through effects only.

8.3 Module format

Little-endian binary. Section order is fixed. The module hash is the SHA-256 of the whole byte string and is stored with every run.

magic            "FFBC"                     4 bytes
format_version   u16                        // this document: 1
flags            u16                        // bit 1 (value 2): ANNOTS after META; other bits 0 (42.1)
section CONSTS   u32 count, then (tag u8, len u32, bytes)       // tags 1 Int 2 Float 3 String 4 Duration 5 Time (42.1)
section TYPES    u32 count, then record and enum layouts        // kind u8, name u32, then per kind (42.1)
section FUNCS    u32 count, then:
                   name_idx u32, arity u8, nlocals u16, ncaps u8,
                   code_len u32, code bytes,
                   nspans u32, then (code_offset u32, source_offset u32, len u32)*
section EFFECTS  u16 declared effect mask, per-workflow
section TOOLS    u16 count, then (tool_name_idx u32, spec_sha256 32 bytes)*
section META     compiler_version, source_sha256, program_policy_sha256, workflow_name   // strings u32 len + bytes; ANNOTS follows if flagged (42.1)
entry            u16 function index of the workflow body

8.4 Instruction set

Operands follow the opcode. u8, u16 are unsigned little-endian; i32 is a signed jump offset relative to the next instruction.

HexMnemonicOperandsStack effect and behavior
00NOP
01CONSTk:u16push constant k
02-04UNIT, TRUE, FALSEpush
05POPdiscard top
06DUPduplicate top
10LOAD_LOCALi:u16push local i
11STORE_LOCALi:u16pop into local i (each local assigned once; verifier enforces)
12LOAD_CAPi:u8push captured value i of the current closure
20-25ADD_I, SUB_I, MUL_I, DIV_I, MOD_I, NEG_Ichecked i64; division by zero raises div_zero
26-2AADD_F, SUB_F, MUL_F, DIV_F, NEG_Ff64
2BCONCATstring concatenation
30-35EQ, NE, LT, LE, GT, GEstructural comparison of two values of the same static type
36NOTboolean negation
40JUMPoff:i32forward jump only
41JUMP_IF_FALSEoff:i32pop bool; jump if false (also used for &&, || short-circuit)
42LOOPoff:i32backward jump; the only backward jump; costs extra fuel
50CALLfn:u16, argc:u8push frame; args become locals
51CALL_CLOSUREargc:u8closure below args
52RETpop frame, push result
53CLOSUREfn:u16, ncaps:u8pop ncaps values, push closure
60MAKE_LISTn:u16pop n, push list
61MAKE_MAPn:u16pop 2n (key, value), push map
62MAKE_RECtype:u16, n:u8pop n fields
63GET_FIELDf:u8record field by index
64INDEXlist or map lookup; out of range raises index_oob
65NEW_LISTpush empty builder list
66LIST_PUSHpop value, append to list below it
67LIST_LENpush length
68LIST_GETpop index, list; push element (unchecked after verified loop bounds)
70MAKE_ENUMtype:u16, variant:u8, n:u8pop n payload values
71TEST_VARIANTvariant:u8peek enum, push bool
72UNPACKn:u8pop enum, push its n payload values
73TRYif top is Err or None return it from the current function (or fail the run in a workflow body); else replace with payload
78INTERPn:u8pop n displayable values, push string
80BUILTINid:u16, argc:u8pure native function; cost proportional to input size
90EFFECTkind:u8, argc:u8suspends the VM with Suspended{seq, kind, args}; on resume pushes the recorded result
91PUSH_COMPfn:u16, ncaps:u8register a compensation closure (with it bound at completion time)
F0HALTcomplete with top of stack
F1FAILcode:u16fail the run with a code from the failure table

Effect kinds for EFFECT: 1 tool_call, 2 scope_check, 3 approval, 4 sleep, 5 agent_call, 6 execute_plan, 7 emit_alert, 8 now, 9 log, 10 par_map, 11 embed (text, model id and version in; vector or blob reference out; recorded), 12 similarity_query (query and parameters in; ranked entity ids with the index watermark out; recorded).

8.5 Lowering rules (compiler obligations)

SourceLowering
a |> f(x)Desugared to f(a, x) at parse time.
e?Evaluate e, then TRY.
matchEvaluate scrutinee into a temp local; for each arm TEST_VARIANT, JUMP_IF_FALSE to next arm, UNPACK and bind, evaluate guard (skip to next arm on false), evaluate body, JUMP to end. The checker guarantees exhaustiveness, so the final fall-through is FAIL match_failed and unreachable in verified code.
for x in xs { body }NEW index local i = 0; LIST_LEN; loop: compare, JUMP_IF_FALSE to end, LIST_GET, bind, body, increment, LOOP.
map, filter, filter_map, flat_mapSame loop shape with a result builder (NEW_LIST, LIST_PUSH). Closures are inlined when they have no captures that escape, otherwise CALL_CLOSURE.
par_map(xs, f, limit)One EFFECT par_map with args (list, closure, limit, on_error).
let x = e compensate cEvaluate e and store x; build closure for c capturing x as it; PUSH_COMP.
require approval(...)Evaluate args, EFFECT approval, TRY with failure code approval_denied.
String interpolationEvaluate pieces, INTERP n.

8.6 Effect protocol, replay and parallelism

  1. On EFFECT, the VM stops with Suspended{ seq, kind, args }. seq is a per-run counter assigned by the VM, incremented at each effect in program order. Effects inside par_map children use a hierarchical id parent_seq/task_index/child_seq.
  2. The engine records effect_scheduled (with seq, kind, args_hash, canonical args) before it performs the effect, and effect_completed or effect_failed with the result afterwards.
  3. Replay: to rebuild state, start from the beginning (or the last snapshot) and, at each suspension, look up seq in the log. If found and the VM's args_hash matches, resume with the recorded result without performing the effect. If the hash differs, fail the run with nondeterminism_detected. If not found, this is the live frontier: perform the effect.
  4. par_map: the engine starts one child VM per element (up to limit concurrently), each running the closure with its own hierarchical effect ids. Results are joined in input order. With on_error = fail (default) the first failure cancels the siblings and the effect fails; with collect the result is List<Result<B, E>>.
  5. Non-deterministic inputs (now, agent output, tool results, approval decisions) enter only as recorded effect results. Randomness is not offered; use a recorded effect if needed.

8.7 Fuel, limits and snapshots

ItemRule
FuelEach instruction costs 1; LOOP costs 5; EFFECT costs 50; BUILTIN costs 1 + n/16 where n is the total input element or byte count. Per-run limit set by the engine (default 50,000,000); agent plans execute with a smaller per-plan limit (default 5,000,000).
LimitsValue stack 65,536; call depth 256; live heap 64 MiB (counted by value size); string 1 MiB; list 1,000,000 elements; module 4 MiB; at most 10,000 effects per run unless the workflow declares #[effects(max = N)] up to 1,000,000.
Failure codesarith_overflow, arith_nan, div_zero, index_oob, match_failed, unwrap_failed, approval_denied, scope_denied, effect_failed, timeout, budget_exceeded, out_of_fuel, nondeterminism_detected, limit_exceeded, cancelled, numbered 1 to 15 in this order, and type_mismatch (16, 42.1)
Snapshot(module_hash, pc, frames[], stack[], compensation_stack[], next_seq, heap) encoded as deterministic CBOR (RFC 8949 section 4.2). A snapshot is a cache of replay: dropping all snapshots never changes behavior. Taken every 500 effects or 5 minutes, whichever is first.

8.8 Verifier (MUST run before load)

  1. All jump targets lie inside the function and on instruction boundaries. Only LOOP jumps backward.
  2. Abstract stack depth is the same on every path that reaches an instruction, never negative, and never above 65,536 minus nlocals (the module stores no per-function maximum; the verifier computes each function's, 42.1).
  3. Local indices are below nlocals; each local is written before read on every path and written at most once.
  4. Every function ends in RET, HALT or FAIL; no fall-through off the end.
  5. CALL arity equals the callee's; CONST, MAKE_REC, MAKE_ENUM indices exist; effect kinds are known.
  6. Every tool referenced has an entry in TOOLS whose spec_sha256 matches the registry at load time. A mismatch rejects the module (the tool changed since compile).
  7. Declared effect mask is a superset of the effects of all reachable EFFECT instructions.

9. Engine

9.1 Workflow lifecycle

  1. Register: submit .ff source for a workflow name. The engine runs the front end (lex, parse, resolve, type and effect check) and, when a program is given, the policy check (section 7.5, rule 7). The result is a list of diagnostics.
  2. Compile and verify: with no errors, produce a module (section 8.3) and run the verifier (section 8.8).
  3. Publish: stores an immutable workflow_version with source, module, module hash, inferred effect set and tool spec hashes. Versions are integers per workflow, assigned in order.
  4. Deprecate: blocks new runs from starting on that version; existing runs continue.

9.2 Run state machine

StatusMeaningEnters fromLeaves to
pendingSubmitted, not yet leasedsubmitrunning, cancelled
runningA worker holds the lease and the VM is executing or an effect is in flightpending, waiting, pausedwaiting, paused, compensating, succeeded, failed, cancelling
waitingSuspended on an effect that needs time: approval, timer, retry backoff, rate-limit delayrunningrunning, cancelling, failed (timeout)
pausedStopped by a scope version change or kill switch; no effects executerunning, waitingrunning, cancelling
cancellingCancel requested; in-flight effects are being cancelledany non-terminalcompensating, cancelled
compensatingRunning registered compensations in reverse orderrunning (on failure), cancellingfailed, cancelled
succeeded, failed, cancelledTerminal. failed and cancelled carry compensation_outcome: none, completed or failednone

Status is a projection of the event log and is also stored on the runs row for querying. If they disagree, the log wins and the row is repaired.

9.3 Event log

Each event is { run_id, seq, type, at, actor, payload }. seq starts at 1 and is gapless per run. actor is engine, vm, tool:<name>, or user:<id>.

TypePayload (key fields)
run_submittedworkflow_version_id, program_id, scope_version, input, idempotency_key, submitted_by
run_startedmodule_sha256, fuel_limit
effect_scheduledpath, kind, args_hash, args
scope_decisionpath, decision object (section 4.3)
effect_startedpath, attempt, executor
effect_completedpath, result, duration_ms
effect_failedpath, attempt, error {code, message, retryable}
effect_retry_scheduledpath, attempt, retry_at
effect_outcome_unknownpath (a non-idempotent effect lost its executor mid-flight)
observation_ingestedpath, observation_id, entity_count, edge_count
approval_requestedpath, approval_id, role, reason, proposed, expires_at
approval_decidedapproval_id, decision (approved | denied), actor, comment
approval_expiredapproval_id
timer_started, timer_firedpath, fire_at
agent_call_recordedpath, model, prompt_ref, response_ref, tokens_in, tokens_out, latency_ms
plan_proposed, plan_accepted, plan_rejectedplan_id, source_sha256, diagnostics[] or module_sha256
child_run_started, child_run_finishedchild_run_id, status
budget_exceededresource (tokens, tool_calls, requests, fuel), limit, used
scope_changed, run_paused, run_resumedold_version, new_version, action; reason
compensation_started, compensation_step, compensation_finishedcount; index, name, result; outcome
snapshot_takenat_seq, snapshot_id
alert_emitted, logseverity, message; level, message (capped at 4 KiB)
run_cancel_requested, run_cancelledactor, reason
run_succeeded, run_failedoutput; code, message

9.4 Scheduling and single-writer guarantee

At most one worker advances a run at a time. The mechanism is a lease with a fencing token:

loop:
  run = SELECT ... FROM runs
        WHERE status IN ('pending','running','waiting') AND next_wake_at <= now()
          AND (lease_expires_at IS NULL OR lease_expires_at < now())
        ORDER BY tenant_fair_rank, priority DESC, next_wake_at
        LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED
  UPDATE runs SET lease_owner = me, lease_expires_at = now() + 30s, fence = fence + 1 RETURNING fence
  state = restore(latest snapshot) or fresh VM
  events = read events after snapshot.at_seq
  advance VM using replay rules (section 8.6)
  while VM suspends at live frontier:
      perform effect (9.5); every append uses:  INSERT INTO run_events ... WHERE runs.fence = :fence
      renew lease every 10s
  on VM completion/failure: append terminal event, release lease
  on wait (approval/timer): set next_wake_at, release lease
  • An append whose fence no longer matches fails, and the old worker MUST stop immediately (it lost the lease).
  • Fairness: tenant_fair_rank is a weighted round-robin position per tenant so one tenant's backlog cannot starve others. A per-tenant cap on concurrently running runs applies (default 20).
  • Wakeups: a timer firing, an effect completing out of band, an approval decision, a cancel request, or a scope change all set next_wake_at = now() and emit a notification on the engine_wake channel (Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY, or Pub/Sub) so workers need not poll quickly.

9.5 Executing effects

KindBehavior
scope_checkCall ScopeService.Check; record scope_decision; result is Ok(proof) or Err(ScopeDenied).
tool_callResolve the tool spec; verify InScope arguments have recorded allow decisions; call the limiter (section 14); call ToolRunner.Execute with an idempotency key; forward streamed observations to the graph; complete with the tool's declared output value.
approvalCreate an approval row, record approval_requested, set the run waiting with next_wake_at = expires_at. A decision or expiry wakes the run.
sleepCreate a timer; wait.
agent_callAssemble context (section 16), call the model, store prompt and response blobs, record agent_call_recorded; on a plan, compile and check it, then record plan_proposed and either plan_accepted or plan_rejected.
execute_planStart a child run on the accepted module, inheriting program, scope version and the remaining budget; complete when the child finishes.
par_mapStart child task VMs with hierarchical paths; join in input order.
emit_alertRecord alert_emitted and write an outbox event to run.events.

Retries

delay(n) = min(max_ms, initial_ms * multiplier^(n-1))         // n = failed attempts so far
wait    = random_uniform(0, delay(n))                          // "full jitter"
retry only if error.retryable and attempt < max_attempts and run deadline not passed

Errors are retryable when: transport unavailable, deadline exceeded, HTTP 429 or 5xx from a tool's upstream, and explicit tool-reported transient errors. Permanent: scope denied, validation errors, 4xx other than 429, budget exceeded. A rate-limit denial is not a failure: the engine waits retry_after and tries again without counting an attempt.

Idempotency and at-least-once execution

  • Every effect has idempotency_key = hex(sha256(run_id || "/" || path)), stable across retries and across crashes. Tools that talk to external systems MUST pass it through.
  • Tools marked idempotent: true may be re-executed after an ambiguous crash. For others, the engine records effect_outcome_unknown and moves the run to waiting until an operator resolves it, or the workflow declares #[on_unknown(retry)].

9.6 Compensation (saga)

  1. Each PUSH_COMP instruction pushes a closure onto the run's compensation stack (part of VM state and snapshots).
  2. On failure or cancellation, the engine appends compensation_started and runs the closures in reverse order, each as normal effects (recorded, retried, idempotent).
  3. If a compensation fails permanently, record compensation_step with the error, continue with the remaining ones, and set compensation_outcome = failed. Never abandon the stack silently.
  4. Compensations never receive new approval gates unless their own effect class requires one; the original approval stays valid for reversing the same action.

9.7 Approvals

  • An approval is addressed to a role within the tenant (and optionally a program). Any user with that role and the permission approvals:decide may decide.
  • The requester of the run MUST NOT approve their own run's approval when the run was started by a user (reason: self_approval). Runs started by schedules or service accounts have no such constraint.
  • Decisions are idempotent per (approval, actor); the first decision wins; later attempts get 409.
  • Expiry: at expires_at the approval becomes expired and the effect fails with approval_denied unless an escalation role was declared, in which case a new approval is created for that role.

9.8 Cancellation, timeouts, deadlines

  • CancelRun appends run_cancel_requested. The worker cancels in-flight effects (runner streams get a cancel signal; adapters stop within 2 s), then runs compensations, then appends run_cancelled.
  • #[timeout(d)] on a workflow sets a run deadline; on a let-statement it bounds that effect or block. Expiry fails with code timeout.

9.9 Crash recovery

  1. A leased run whose lease expired is picked up by any worker (section 9.4).
  2. The new worker restores the latest snapshot (if any), replays the events after it, and reaches the live frontier.
  3. For the effect at the frontier: if effect_started exists without a terminal event, treat as ambiguous (section 9.5). If only effect_scheduled exists, perform it.

9.10 Submit idempotency

SubmitRun requires an idempotency key. The pair (tenant, key) is unique. A repeat with the same request_hash returns the original run; a repeat with a different hash returns ALREADY_EXISTS reason IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED. Keys are retained for 7 days.

10. SQL data model (PostgreSQL 15+)

Each service owns one schema and reaches other schemas only through its API. There are no foreign keys across schemas; IDs are stored by value and validated by the owning service. Extensions: pg_trgm, citext.

10.1 Entity overview

identitytenants, users, memberships, invites, email_verifications, refresh_tokens, api_keys, signing_keys, auth_audit
scopeprograms, scope_versions, scope_decisions, kill_switches
enginetools, workflows, workflow_versions, runs, run_events, effects, timers, approvals, snapshots, outbox
graphentities, edges, entity_tags, edge_tags, observations, change_events, snapshots, embeddings, vector_indexes, vector_segments (the last four are defined in sections 21.6 and 22.9)
notifieralert_rules, channels, alert_events, deliveries, processed_events

identity.users 1—* identity.refresh_tokens          identity.tenants 1—* identity.api_keys

engine.workflows 1—* engine.workflow_versions       engine.workflow_versions 1—* engine.runs

engine.runs 1—* engine.approvals                    engine.runs 1—* engine.timers
