33.7 Secrets and redaction

  • Detection is by named, structured rules: aws_access_key_id, aws_secret_access_key, github_token, github_fine_grained_pat, gitlab_token, slack_token, slack_webhook (https://hooks.slack.com/services/ followed by any three /-separated alphanumeric segments: Slack issues T…/B…/ and a 24-character secret, but the scan checks neither the prefixes nor the lengths, which redacts more, never less), stripe_secret_key, google_api_key, openai_api_key, anthropic_api_key, sendgrid_api_key, npm_token, azure_storage_key, jwt, private_key, connection_string (password only), url_credentials (password only). Bare entropy (generic_high_entropy) is a low-confidence tier: never a finding on its own, still redacted.
  • Findings carry the rule id, the redacted preview (28.3) and the keyed-hash input; the HMAC is computed by the caller with the tenant's hash data key. Observe emits exposed_secret tags only with a keyed hasher.
  • Redaction before storage and indexing: credential-shaped strings are replaced by [REDACTED:<rule>:<shape>:<length>] before a hunk or normalized snapshot is hashed for the index, indexed, embedded or logged. The original bytes stay only in the encrypted findings and the encrypted seals of the raw snapshot (34.4). Redact rescans its output until a scan is empty, so it is idempotent (CONTENT-INV-10). Hidden form fields keep only short numeric or boolean values (CONTENT-INV-04).

33.8 Collection integrity and coverage

  • Integrity flags degrade a capture: fetch_failed, truncated, login_redirect, challenge_page (strong markers such as challenge platforms degrade alone; weak ones, a CAPTCHA widget, "access denied", only with status 403, 429 or 503), error_after_success, soft_error_after_success (a title naming an HTTP error or maintenance on a 2xx page after a 2xx page that did not), mass_removal (more than half of at least four previous features gone), catch_all (the same body, SimHash Hamming ≤ 3, at two other URLs of the host). A degraded capture gets no diff and no withdrawal, emits only its source and redirects, is reported as a collection problem and updates source health (CONTENT-INV-06).
  • Coverage is complete iff the outcome is complete, the body normalized completely, every extractor that ran graded complete and none applicable is canary-disabled (CONTENT-INV-14); failed for degraded or failed captures; unsupported for binary bodies; partial otherwise. covered_rules lists the families read completely; a complete HTML reading also covers the inline-script and inline-style families; header and redirect families are covered whenever the capture is complete. HTML parse errors do not lower the grade (the algorithm recovers from all of them); only its limits do. Any JavaScript diagnostic lowers the grade to partial.

33.9 Lineage and modules

  • File-level lineage: for a script or style with no own predecessor, the best predecessor among the page's previously loaded scripts (and, in the store, the host's open versions of the same kind, 34.1). A unique lineage key (the URL with hash-like segments stripped) decides first; otherwise SimHash over 3-token shingles of the fingerprint tokens (bound identifiers erased to $, free identifiers and property names kept) at Hamming distance ≤ 6 (DefaultMaxHamming), ties broken by size ratio, then URL. Matchers simhash (default), minhash and jaccard (minimum Jaccard 0.75) are selectable; the scan is linear (exact at any threshold). A match emits previous_version and the diff is computed against the predecessor (CONTENT-INV-08).
  • Module-level lineage (level 2.5): bundles are split into bundler modules (webpack tables, esbuild wrappers, rollup/vite top-level statements; strategy auto by default) and each normalized module is hashed with locally bound identifiers renamed to positional names. An unchanged module is no change wherever it moved; a bundle whose modules all match is reordered (CONTENT-INV-09). Vendor modules (node_modules/ ids, configured hashes, a library banner inside) are suppressed and a library change is reported as a version change by js.libraries. Module changes are not level 3: they appear in the summary and text diff.

33.10 Diff and classification

  • Levels: hash (bytes differ), text (normalized hunks differ), semantic (the feature-set difference, computed over the whole capture with its derived documents, so a feature moving between documents is no change). Level-3 changes map to graph changes.
  • Outcomes (counted): no_change, normalized_equal, masked_only, reordered, structural (text only), changed (level 3), degraded (no diff). masked_only and reordered are not changes.
  • Class: semantic_endpoint (endpoint, form, parameter, GraphQL operation), metadata (headers only), observation (other level 3), structural (text only). catalog (a new advisory against an unchanged library) is reserved for the advisory matcher (D4, v3).
  • Newness: likely_new against a complete previous capture, first_seen_only without one, pre_existing for the baseline and for returns (needs the store's feature-id history).
  • Cause: target as soon as one raw body differs; otherwise normalizer if a normalizer version or rule table differs, else parser if the extractor set differs. Default feeds and alerts hide non-target causes (CONTENT-INV-15).
  • Baseline: the first complete capture of a (URL, profile) is baseline = true: it produces graph data but no feed items or alerts.
  • Diff policy: size (default; Myers linear space on tokens up to 16 KiB of normalized text, histogram on lines above), myers, histogram, patience, all through BoundedDiff (30.2).
  • Re-derive (normative; contentstore.Store.Rederive, G-CST-04): upgrading a parser, normalizer, extractor or rule table changes the derivation stamp (34.2); the maintenance sweep re-derives the stored snapshots from their raw bodies, predecessor first (an ingest re-derives a stale predecessor before diffing against it), rewriting their normalized text, features and derived columns in place and recomputing the diffs between snapshots of the new derivation (a diff whose other side is still stale keeps its old content until that side is re-derived; the diff into a re-derived snapshot is always recomputed). Re-derivation is not a change: it records no diff, event or alert and keeps every id and sequence number (contentstore OPEN_QUESTIONS 11; CSTORE-INV-14..16). cause = parser or normalizer is what Compare reports when two derivations of identical bodies are diffed on demand.
  • Noise metric: false_change_rate per program, text-level changes with zero level-3 changes on pages whose features are stable; target below 5 % (NoiseCounter).

33.11 Scores: rank, never send

A change's score in [0, 1] and its score_features (named, weighted reasons) are stored with it, so every rank is explainable. The model is additive and hand-set. Contributions are summed per reason name, the sum clamped to [−1, 1] and rounded to 4 decimals; a reason whose weight rounds to 0 is dropped (it is not stored); reasons are sorted by name; the score is the sum of the reason weights in that order, clamped to [0, 1] and rounded to 4 decimals. Weights:

Feature kind (added)ReasonWeightFeature kind (added)ReasonWeight
secretexposed_secret0.9scriptnew_script0.1
source_disclosuresource_disclosure0.85library, framenew_library, new_frame0.1
endpoint, schema_endpointnew_endpoint0.4parameternew_parameter0.1
formnew_form0.3module_importnew_module_import0.05
sourcemapsource_map_exposed0.3header, robots_rule, redirectheader_added, robots_rule_added, new_redirect0.05
graphql_operationnew_graphql_operation0.2style, css_import, link, sitemap_entry, security_fieldnew_style, new_link, securitytxt_changed0.02
  • Special: state_changing_method 0.15 (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE on a new endpoint or form); templated_path 0.1 (a {id}, {uuid}, {hex} or {expr} key); third_party_script_without_sri 0.25; removed_security_header 0.35 (CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy, COOP, COEP, CORP); csp_loosened 0.3; cookie_flags_weakened 0.25; library_version_change 0.2; sri_removed 0.25; header_changed 0.1.
  • Word boosts, once each per change set, on a changed endpoint, form, link, script, parameter or GraphQL operation key: word_admin 0.15 (admin, administrator, manage), word_auth 0.1 (auth, login, oauth, token, session, password), word_payment 0.15 (pay, billing, checkout, invoice, stripe), word_internal 0.15 (internal, private, staging, intranet), word_debug 0.15 (debug, trace, test, dev).
  • Removals count at −½ of the positive weight (<reason>_removed), returns at −¼; a change made only of removals or returns scores 0.
Scores rank, never send. No code path leads from a score to a request: the scoring files import only pure standard-library packages (CONTENT-INV-11), alert rules cannot read diff.score (A0202, 36.5), and scoring changes no fetch count. A learned ranker is v3.

33.12 Decoded blobs for the index

DecodeBlobs(text) returns the text inside base64 (standard or URL-safe alphabet), hex, gzip, zlib and zstd blobs found in content, nested up to three levels (path for example base64>gzip; through codec.DecodeLayers, bounded by 8 MiB decoded and 200 layers; a text layer that only encodes a deeper one, such as base64 of base64, is skipped for that layer), with the outer span and the encoding path, redacted for every secret rule. Blobs inside secrets (JWT segments) are never decoded; binary payloads and plain text give nothing (CONTENT-INV-17). It is used by the content store's indexing step only, not by Process; secrets found inside decoded blobs are redacted but not reported as findings.

34. Content store

34.1 Ingest

IngestRequest{program, Capture, entity, run, effect_path, page_url}
  signature = SHA-256(body hash, status, media type, headers@1 block of every hop)
  ├─ same (url, profile, signature, fetched_at) already stored ─▶ duplicate: nothing written
  ├─ the open version has the signature ─▶ unchanged: last_seen moves, the capture is counted and
  │                                        the open version's observation is re-published with the
  │                                        re-fetch's fetch time (as processing the re-fetch afresh would
  │                                        give, byte for byte: CSTORE-INV-13)
  ├─ older than the open version ─▶ CAPTURE_OUT_OF_ORDER
  └─ new version
       content.Process(learned volatility, tenant keyed hasher, previous capture, host bodies, page url)
       lineage: no own predecessor and a script or style ─▶ FindPredecessor over the page's previous
                scripts and the host's open versions of the kind (at most 64, minus those sharing a
                lineage key with one of the page's scripts)
       content.Compare(predecessor, cur)   (a baseline diffs every artifact against the empty document)
       blobs: redact body and header values (spans sealed) ─▶ blob codec ─▶ tenant chunk store
       one transaction: open-version check, exact quota check, close the parent, snapshot row, diff row
                        (seq), findings (encrypted), volatility, usage, outbox event
       then: retention of the key, inline indexing

Degraded captures are stored closed with an empty validity interval, get no diff and never become the open version. Validity is [valid_from, valid_to) per (URL, profile), contiguous and disjoint with one open version, so an as-of lookup is one index probe (CSTORE-INV-09). Versions cited by a reviewed or alerted change are pinned and skipped by retention.

34.2 Schema (migrations 0031 and 0032, schema content)

Owned by role ff_content, subject to RLS: every table with tenant_id gets forced RLS and the tenant_isolation policy; the store sets app.tenant_id in every transaction; only content.programs has the cross-tenant svc_ff_content policy (background sweeps enumerate tenants there). The *_ref and *_manifest columns hold chunk-store ManifestIDs (hex) of the tenant's chunk store.

CREATE TABLE content.programs (tenant_id uuid, program_id uuid, diff_seq bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, program_id));

CREATE TABLE content.snapshots (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY, tenant_id uuid NOT NULL, program_id uuid NOT NULL,
  entity_id text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', url text NOT NULL, url_template text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  host text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', profile text NOT NULL, run_id text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  effect_path text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', fetched_at timestamptz NOT NULL, last_seen timestamptz NOT NULL,
  valid_from timestamptz NOT NULL, valid_to timestamptz,          -- NULL: the open version
  status int NOT NULL, mime text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', kind text NOT NULL, signature text NOT NULL,
  headers_ref text NOT NULL, body_manifest text NOT NULL, body_sha256 text NOT NULL,
  raw_size bigint NOT NULL, stored_size bigint NOT NULL,
  normalizer_version text NOT NULL, normalized_manifest text NOT NULL,
  parent_id uuid REFERENCES content.snapshots (id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  lineage_parent_id uuid REFERENCES content.snapshots (id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  features_ref text NOT NULL, outcome text NOT NULL, coverage text NOT NULL,
  degraded boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, simhash bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  pinned boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, pin_reason text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  codec text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'chunk', delta_base uuid REFERENCES content.snapshots (id),
  delta_depth int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, body_seal bytea, headers_seal bytea, key_id text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT snapshots_validity CHECK (valid_to IS NULL OR valid_to >= valid_from),
  CONSTRAINT snapshots_codec CHECK (codec IN ('chunk', 'delta')),
  CONSTRAINT snapshots_degraded_closed CHECK (NOT degraded OR valid_to IS NOT NULL));
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX snapshots_open ON content.snapshots (tenant_id, program_id, url, profile) WHERE valid_to IS NULL;
-- plus snapshots_key, _program, _host_open, _signature, _delta_base, _parent, _lineage_parent

CREATE TABLE content.diffs (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY, tenant_id uuid NOT NULL, program_id uuid NOT NULL, seq bigint NOT NULL,
  from_snapshot uuid REFERENCES content.snapshots (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  to_snapshot uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES content.snapshots (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  levels text[] NOT NULL, algorithm text NOT NULL, normalizer_version text NOT NULL, stats jsonb NOT NULL,
  hunks_ref text NOT NULL, semantic_ref text NOT NULL, stored_size bigint NOT NULL, index_docs int NOT NULL,
  at timestamptz NOT NULL, url text NOT NULL, profile text NOT NULL,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), UNIQUE (tenant_id, program_id, seq));

CREATE TABLE content.findings (                                   -- secrets, encrypted at rest
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY, tenant_id uuid NOT NULL, program_id uuid NOT NULL,
  snapshot_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES content.snapshots (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  rule_id text NOT NULL, preview text NOT NULL, keyed_hash text NOT NULL,
  ciphertext bytea NOT NULL, key_id text NOT NULL, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now());

CREATE TABLE content.volatility (tenant_id uuid, program_id uuid, url_template text, profile text,
  feature_path text, changes int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, stable_observations int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  streak int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, frozen_version int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, program_id, url_template, profile, feature_path));

CREATE TABLE content.quota_limits (tenant_id uuid PRIMARY KEY, captures_per_day bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  stored_bytes bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, index_bytes bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0);   -- 0 = unlimited
CREATE TABLE content.quota_usage (tenant_id uuid PRIMARY KEY, day text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  captures bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, stored_bytes bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, index_bytes bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0);
CREATE TABLE content.index_state (tenant_id uuid, program_id uuid, watermark bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  docs bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, bytes bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, program_id));
CREATE TABLE content.data_keys (tenant_id uuid, purpose text, version int, kek_id text NOT NULL,
  wrapped bytea NOT NULL, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, purpose, version));
CREATE TABLE content.outbox (id bigserial PRIMARY KEY, event_id uuid NOT NULL UNIQUE, topic text NOT NULL,
  ordering_key text, payload jsonb NOT NULL, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), published_at timestamptz);

-- 0032_content_rederive: the pipeline stamp that derived each snapshot (re-derive, 33.10)
ALTER TABLE content.snapshots ADD COLUMN derive_stamp text NOT NULL DEFAULT '';
CREATE INDEX snapshots_derive ON content.snapshots (tenant_id, program_id, valid_from, id, derive_stamp);

derive_stamp names the derivation (contentstore.DerivationStamp: a SHA-256 over every normalizer’s version and rule-table hash and every extractor’s version); a snapshot whose stamp is not the running pipeline’s is re-derived from its stored raw body. Rows from before 0032 have the empty stamp, so the first pass visits each once and, with an unchanged pipeline, writes only the stamps (CSTORE-INV-14). The index serves the job’s ordered scan of one program in (valid_from, id) order and filters fresh rows inside the index.

(Column types and nullability as in the migration files, which are authoritative; the abbreviated tables above omit NOT NULL on composite key columns.)

34.3 Records, blob codecs and ids

ColumnBlob content
headers_refcapture record: URL, profile, status, headers and redirect chain with header values redacted (canonical JSON)
body_manifestthe body with credential-shaped spans replaced by the redaction placeholder
normalized_manifestthe main artifact's normalized text (redacted)
features_refthe processed capture without texts and derived bodies, the other artifacts' normalized texts, and the feature-id history (for "returned")
hunks_refunified hunks by reference ({"h": header, "o": "c3 d2 i1"}, lines read back from the two snapshots' normalized texts; verbatim when that does not reproduce them); none for a baseline
semantic_refthe change set without hunks, side-by-side rows and per-artifact feature changes
  • Blob codecs (codec column): delta (default): each blob is an FFZB frame (31.7) whose raw dictionary is the delta base's same blob (parent or lineage parent) and, for the normalized text and features, this snapshot's body and normalized text; chains are bounded by KeyframeInterval (16), and retention re-encodes a deleted base's dependents as keyframes first. chunk: blobs stored plain, relying on FastCDC dedupe and per-chunk zstd.
  • Records are canonical JSON. Derived ids are UUID version 8: SHA-256 over uvarint(len) ‖ bytes of the domain and each part, first 16 bytes, version nibble 8, variant 10, so every replica and replay derives the same ids (CSTORE-INV-04).
  • No cross-tenant dedupe: one chunk-store namespace per tenant (CSTORE-INV-02).

34.4 Encryption envelope

  • Each tenant has data keys (32 random bytes) per purpose: enc (AES-256-GCM for findings and redaction seals) and hash (HMAC-SHA256 keyed hashes of secrets, so they survive an enc rotation). Keys are stored only wrapped by the key-encryption key in content.data_keys (purpose, version, kek_id).
  • KEK port KeyEncrypter{ID, Wrap(tenant, dek), Unwrap(tenant, wrapped)}: an implementation MUST bind the wrapped key to the tenant (additional data). LocalKEK (development, from FF_CONTENT_KEK) wraps as 0x01 ‖ nonce(12) ‖ ciphertext ‖ tag with additional data "ff-content-kek|" ‖ tenant UUID; a Cloud KMS adapter (Encrypt/Decrypt with the same additional data) fits the same port and is v3 work (G-DOC-10).
  • Ciphertexts: 0x01 ‖ nonce(12) ‖ ciphertext ‖ tag(16), nonce from two Rng draws (little-endian u64, then the low 4 bytes of the second). Additional data: "ff-content-seal|<tenant>|<snapshot>|body" or "...|headers" for seals (the redacted spans), "ff-content-finding|<tenant>|<snapshot>|<finding>" for findings (UUIDs in canonical text).
  • Only the preview and keyed hash are in the clear and searchable. Raw reads (raw = true, admins only) are the only path that restores secrets (CSTORE-INV-07).

34.5 Retention and GC

Per (URL, profile) key, keep the newest KeepLast (50) ∪ fetched within KeepFor (180 days) ∪ pinned ∪ the newest (open) version; diffs touching a deleted snapshot go with it (cascade) and their index documents are tombstoned. Retention then runs chunk GC over the tenant's live and pinned manifests and sets the stored-bytes usage to the store's physical record bytes (CSTORE-INV-03). GC MAY recompress at a higher zstd level and keeps the result only when it saves at least 1/8.

34.6 Quotas

Per tenant, zero meaning unlimited (content.quota_limits): captures per UTC day, stored bytes, index bytes. Checked in the write transaction on a locked usage row: a capture is admitted iff today's count is below the limit, stored + added ≤ limit and the index is below its limit; otherwise QUOTA_EXCEEDED with meta.quota naming the quota and, for the daily quota, retry_after until midnight UTC, and nothing is written (chunks put by the refused capture are reclaimed by GC). Duplicates are free; concurrent captures under limit N admit exactly N (CSTORE-INV-06). There are no default limits: operators set them per tenant (GetQuotaStatus reports them).

34.7 Search integration and defaults

  • One textindex index per (tenant, program). Schema: text fields text, url, decoded; keyword fields program, url_template, kind (hunk, <feature>_<op> such as endpoint_added, snapshot), side (added, removed, modified), feature, mime, vendor, class, newness, cause, profile, baseline, decoded_from; numeric at (Unix ms).
  • Documents: per hunk its removed and added sides separately; per level-3 change one record; optionally each key's latest normalized snapshot. Text is cut at 64 KiB and redacted; blobs inside it are decoded by DecodeBlobs into decoded with decoded_from naming the encodings.
  • Document ids are seq << 20 | position, so a rebuild from the stored diffs reproduces the index; writes use Update, so re-indexing after a crash is idempotent; the watermark is content.index_state (CSTORE-INV-05). Index modes: inline (default, searchable when ingest returns) or batched.
  • Defaults: vendored bundles (node_modules paths, vendor/framework/polyfills chunk names, all-vendor module sets) are excluded unless the query names vendor or include_vendor is set. FirstAppeared(text) returns the earliest snapshot whose diff (baselines included) added the string: candidates from a phrase query (or a regex for text without word tokens), at most 10,000, verified by exact substring (CSTORE-INV-10). Hybrid mode ships only if it is at least as good as BM25 on recall@k and MRR over the labelled set conformance/textindex/ranking/labelled.json (19 queries; BM25 MRR 0.947, P@1 0.895); it is not enabled in v2.

34.8 Events

Every accepted capture writes one content.changes event (type content.changed or content.captured, ordering key the program) to content.outbox in the ingest transaction (23.7 shape). Its payload carries the capture's oam observation (with authority), the change set's levels, class, newness, cause, baseline, alertable (not a baseline, cause target, not pre-existing, a change) and, for non-baseline changes, an excerpt: at most 40 lines and 2 KiB of the unified hunks, level-3 artifacts first, redacted once more. The graph ingests the observation and records content_changed on the source entity; the notifier alerts on alertable content events only and ignores the graph's own copy, so one change alerts once.

35. Egress: TCP, SOCKS5, DNS and per-target limits

The egress proxy (3.2, 4.4, 4.5) stays the tool runner's only route out. v2 adds every TCP port, SOCKS5, DNS, a per-target rate limit at the network edge, intelligence sources and a notify path.

35.1 Listeners

ListenerProtocolAuthentication
HTTPCONNECT host:port for any TCP port, plain-HTTP forward requests, and the control endpoint POST http://egress.flowforge.invalid/v1/ticketsinternal token in Proxy-Authorization (407 otherwise), decision in X-Flowforge-Decision-Id, ports in X-Flowforge-Ports
SOCKS5RFC 1928 CONNECT only (29.10); BIND and UDP ASSOCIATE get reply 7RFC 1929: username = decision id, password = ticket (the 255-octet limit is too short for an internal token)
DNS (UDP and TCP, one port)RFC 1035 through dnsmsgticket in EDNS option code 65280 (local-use range, RFC 6891 9)

A ticket is issued by the control endpoint after the same validation as a proxied request (token, decision, run, effect path, declared ports, kill switch), is bound to all of them and lives at most 60 s. Tickets are per replica and in memory.

35.2 Authorization of every request, tunnel and query

  1. Authenticate the caller (token or ticket).
  2. Load the decision for the caller's tenant: an allow, at most MaxDecisionAge (1 h) old and of the caller's run when one is sent; reloaded on every use.
  3. The decision must cover (host, port). A URL decision covers its host and its URL's port (tunnels only under an https decision). A host-level decision (Host or IP) covers its host for the ports of the program's port policy (default 80 and 443; FF_EGRESS_PORTS), narrowed to the ports the tool call declared. Anything else is port_not_allowed. Userinfo, a mismatched Host, un-normalizable hosts and IP literals under a name decision are refused (permission attaches to the name, 4.5).
  4. Intelligence sources (35.5).
  5. Kill switches; fail closed when their state is unknown.
  6. Per-target rate limit: GCRA (14.2, limiter) on key rl:edge:h:<program>:<registrable domain or ip> (its own key, not the engine's rl:h:, so a request is not charged twice when both share one Redis) at the scope document's rate (DefaultRate 10 requests/s, burst 20, concurrency 8 when the document sets none), plus a concurrency cap per key held for the request's or tunnel's lifetime. It applies to every HTTP request, CONNECT tunnel and SOCKS5 tunnel, so a tool's internal parallelism cannot exceed the program rate (inv_10 at the network edge). Denials answer 429 with Retry-After and X-Flowforge-Retry-After-Ms (SOCKS5: reply 2) and count ff_limiter_denied_total{level="proxy_host"|"proxy_host_concurrency"}. With FF_REDIS_ADDR the GCRA state is shared by every replica (Redis TIME as the clock); the concurrency cap is per replica.
  7. Dial only the decision's pinned addresses, never re-resolving (DNS rebinding cannot redirect a connection); loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast and unspecified addresses need an explicit cidr rule. Plain-HTTP redirects are re-checked with the scope service.

Every refusal answers 403, 407, 429 or 502 with X-Flowforge-Refused: <reason> (SOCKS5 reply 2, 5 or 7; DNS REFUSED), logs egress.refused and counts ff_egress_refused_total{reason}. Reasons are listed in 40.4.

35.3 DNS forwarder

A query is answered only when it carries a valid ticket whose decision is still an allow, no kill switch is engaged, and the name passes a passive scope check (D3; recorded by the scope service; reverse names are checked as the IP they denote) or is an allowlisted intelligence source under a passive decision (recorded as intel_source). Everything else is REFUSED; ANY is NOTIMP; malformed queries get FORMERR. Records of a CNAME chain are kept only up to the first hop that fails the same check; at most 64 records; UDP answers are cut to the client's EDNS size (at most 1232 octets, 512 without EDNS) with TC set (EncodeTruncated), TCP answers to 16 KiB.

35.4 Passive tools (decision D3)

DNS resolution (public resolvers or authoritative servers) and TLS handshakes for certificate collection are passive. They still require a scope check: the class is passive, the decision is recorded and IPs are pinned (tlscert asks for pins with resolve = true; without pins the proxy refuses the tunnel, no_pinned_ips). Passive only changes which windows and program classes allow them. portscan and anything that sends application payloads stay active.

35.5 Intelligence sources

Passive tools that fetch from public sources (crt.sh, RDAP, Team Cymru) go through the proxy but are checked against an allowlist of intelligence sources, not the program scope: the target is the source, not the program's asset. When the host is on the allowlist and the decision is of class passive, the source's configured or resolved public addresses are dialled and each connection (and each allowlisted DNS name) is recorded in the decision log with reason intel_source; other classes get intel_requires_passive. Redirects must stay on the allowlist. DefaultIntelSources(): crt.sh, data.iana.org, rdap.iana.org, rdap.org, the five RIR RDAP servers, rdap.verisign.com, rdap.publicinterestregistry.org, rdap.identitydigital.services, pubapi.registry.google, rdap.centralnic.com (port 443), whois.cymru.com and asn.cymru.com with subdomains (port 43). FF_EGRESS_INTEL: none (default), default, and/or entries host[:port...][=ip] and *.host.

35.6 Notify path

Webhook and Slack deliveries are not program targets. They carry X-Flowforge-Egress-Purpose: notify instead of a decision id; the proxy then requires the service:notifier identity, CONNECT (https only), a destination on FF_NOTIFY_ALLOWLIST (comma-separated names, .example.com for every name below a domain; empty refuses everything), resolves the host itself and connects only when every address passes the 15.4 SSRF check (public unicast), pinned for the connection. The per-target rate limit does not apply (the notifier throttles itself).

36. Tools and the language surface

36.1 Tools

Tool (version)DSL signatureClassEmits or returns
collect 1.0.0(InScope<Url>, String) -> Snapshot (capture profile name)activeFetch through the proxy with the profile's request shape; every redirect hop re-checked under its own decision (an out-of-scope hop is not followed: the redirect response is the capture); body up to 8 MiB (longer: truncated, outcome partial); stored with IngestCapture. For an HTML page also its scripts, styles and each script's source map (at most 32, each scope-checked, a denied one skipped and never contacted). Returns the page's Snapshot with the subresources in resources
crawl 1.0.0(InScope<Url>, Int) -> List<Url> (fetch budget, at most 500)activeBreadth-first frontier: every URL canonicalized and scope-checked before queueing; robots.txt per origin (2xx parsed, 4xx allow all, 5xx, unreachable or out of scope disallow all; agent token flowforge); links, frames and sitemap entries; revisit policy per (program, URL, profile): adaptive by default (halve on change, double on no change, clamped to [1 h, 7 d], first interval 24 h). Returns every in-scope, robots-allowed URL reached
wellknown 1.0.0(InScope<Host>) -> List<Url>activerobots.txt, /.well-known/security.txt then /security.txt, the sitemaps robots.txt names (else /sitemap.xml; sitemap indexes followed to any depth, at most 10 sitemaps per origin in all), OpenAPI at /openapi.json, /swagger.json, /v3/api-docs, /api-docs, /.well-known/openapi.json, GraphQL GET /graphql?query={__typename}. Probes store 2xx answers whose sniffed kind is the expected one
extract 1.0.0(Snapshot) -> FeaturesreadThe stored features grouped by kind; no graph facts (36.6)
unpack_sourcemap 1.0.0(Snapshot) -> List<Snapshot>readThe #src/<path> originals of a stored source map (or of the map a script names)
diff_latest 1.0.0(Snapshot) -> ChangeSetreadDiffSnapshots against the stored predecessor (parent, lineage parent, or the baseline); no graph facts (36.6)
search_changes 1.0.0(String, Int) -> List<ChangeHit>readSearchChanges (32.2 query language, limit 1-1000)
ctlog 1.0.0(Fqdn) -> List<Fqdn>passivecrt.sh search (intel source): names at or below the input; TLSCertificate (keyed by SHA-256 fingerprint of the downloaded DER, at most 50 per query), common_name / san_dns_name edges
rdap 1.0.0(Host) -> List<Organization>passiveIANA bootstrap (cached 24 h) then registry RDAP: DomainRecord, IPNetRecord, Netblock, Organization (never personal data), registration and contains edges
asn 1.0.0(IpAddress) -> Option<Asn>passiveRDAP and Team Cymru TXT via the DNS forwarder: AutonomousSystem, Netblock, AutnumRecord, IPNetRecord; announces, contains, registration
dnsresolve 2.0.0(Fqdn) -> List<IpAddress>passiveThrough the DNS forwarder: A/AAAA, CNAME (every hop scope-checked), MX (PrefDNSRelation), NS, TXT (DNSRecordProperty)
portscan 2.0.0(InScope<Host>, List<Int>) -> List<Int>activeService + port PortRelation per open port, paced per target; tcpconnect 1.1.0 is an alias
tlscert 1.0.0(InScope<Host>, Int) -> Option<TlsCertificate>passive (D3)TLS handshake through a tunnel under a pinned decision, SNI = host, aborted when the certificates arrive (no application data); Service, port, certificate, TLSCertificate, common_name / san_dns_name
httpprobe 1.0.0(InScope<Host>) -> Option<Service>activeunchanged from v1

Every URL a content tool touches is checked twice: a passive check first (no resolution, so out-of-scope names never reach DNS), then the active one that resolves and pins. A denied URL is streamed as Skipped and never contacted. A (URL, profile) whose open version the calling run already stored is returned without a fetch, so one run never splits a deploy's change across two versions. new_in_run compares the version's run with the calling run.

36.2 Standard-library records

Added to the 7.6 prelude after TaskError (builtin type ids are append-only). Tool results are decoded field by field into these layouts, so adapters emit exactly these fields, in this order.

RecordFields (in order)
Snapshotid: String (snp_...; a derived source-map original is <id>#src/<path>), url: String, profile: String, status: Int, mime: String, kind: String (sniffed), body_sha256: String, raw_size: Int, fetched_at: Time, parent_id: Option<String>, lineage_parent_id: Option<String>, coverage: String, degraded: Bool, new_in_run: Bool, redirects: List<String> (each Location followed, in order), resources: List<Snapshot> (scripts, styles, source maps captured with it)
Featuressnapshot_id: String, url: String, coverage: String, links, scripts, styles, frames, sourcemaps: List<Url> (canonical), endpoints, forms, parameters (graph keys such as ep:POST https://h/p), headers, libraries, secrets (<rule>:<redacted preview>, never the secret): List<String>
ChangeSetsnapshot_id: String, from_snapshot_id: Option<String>, diff_id: Option<String>, url: String, outcome: String, levels: List<String>, semantic: Bool (level 3 present), class: String, newness: String, cause: String, baseline: Bool, alertable: Bool (what default feeds show), score: Float (ranking only), changes: List<String> ("<op> <kind>|<key>"), summary: Map<String, Int> (per-kind counts, plus <kind>_<op>)
ChangeHitscore: Float, at: Time, diff_id: String, snapshot_id: String, url: String, kind: String, side: String, feature: String, class: String, newness: String, cause: String, text: String

36.3 Builtins added in v2 (G-LANG-10)

Pure builtins over the codec package, appended to the 8.4 builtin table (ids are append-only). Decoders return None for invalid input and for bytes that are not UTF-8, since DSL strings are text.

BuiltinSignatureSemantics
base64_decode(s: String) -> Option<String>RFC 4648 lenient: either alphabet, padding optional, ASCII white space skipped
base64_encode(s: String) -> StringRFC 4648 4 standard alphabet, padded
url_decode(s: String) -> Option<String>percent-decoding (RFC 3986 2.1), None on a malformed escape; + stays +
url_encode(s: String) -> StringWHATWG component percent-encode set (encodeURIComponent), upper-case hex
hex_decode(s: String) -> Option<String>base16, either case, white space between pairs skipped
hex_encode(s: String) -> Stringlower-case base16 of the UTF-8 bytes

36.4 Reference monitoring workflow

conformance/workflows/monitor.ff, monitor(roots: List<Url>, hosts: List<Fqdn>, budget: Int) -> List<String>, runs once per deploy: crawl from every root and wellknown per host; collect every discovered URL with profile default through par_map(..., limit = 4, on_error = "collect"); unpack_sourcemap; diff_latest of every version this run stored (new_in_run, not degraded); keep semantic && alertable; order by score (ranking only) and emit_alert each. Every target passes scope.check before it reaches a tool.

36.5 The alert diff variable

Alert rules (15) gain a diff section, present on content events (absent elsewhere; absent values follow the three-valued logic of 15.2):

FieldTypeMeaning
snapshot_id, from_snapshot_id, diff_idStringthe versions and the stored diff
url, profileStringthe capture key
outcome, class, newness, causeString33.10
levelsList<String>hash, text, semantic
semantic, baseline, alertableBoollevel 3 present; first complete capture; default-feed visible
kindsList<String><kind>_<op>, for example endpoint_added
changesList<String><op> <kind>|<key>
summaryStringone-line summary (DiffSummary)
excerptStringredacted unified hunks (34.8)

Example: alert new_endpoint when diff.alertable && "endpoint_added" in diff.kinds -> webhook("w") throttle 1h group_by diff.url. The score and its reasons are deliberately not fields: diff.score is diagnostic A0202 (unknown field). Scores appear in the payload's diff section to rank alerts, but never decide whether one is sent.

36.6 One writer per content fact

The content tools stream no observations. The content service publishes each capture's observation (URL, File, Endpoint, ContentProperty, HTTPHeaderProperty, redirects_to, previous_version, ... with coverage-gated authority) in its content.changes event, so every content fact has one writer and authority is applied once. The raw response is the raw snapshot in the tenant's chunk store, not a raw/ blob.

37. Content API

37.1 ContentService (gRPC, proto/flowforge/v1/content.proto)

RPCPermissionPurpose
IngestCaptureruns:submitStore one capture (idempotent; unchanged re-fetches extend last_seen). Returns the snapshot, diff id, duplicate, unchanged, the change summary (with alertable), the lineage parent URL and the event id
GetSnapshotread; raw needs programs:adminMetadata; optionally the redacted body, normalized text, features JSON and finding previews; raw restores secrets
ListSnapshotsreadPer program, URL, profile; newest first; paged (11.2 paging)
DiffSnapshotsreadUnified and/or side-by-side JSON per artifact; from empty means the stored predecessor (or the baseline)
SearchChanges, FirstAppearedreadSection 32 query language over hunks and change records; the earliest snapshot adding a string
PinSnapshot, UnpinSnapshotalerts:writeRetention pins with a reason

The tenant comes from the internal token; RLS is set per transaction.

37.2 REST (gateway)

RouteParametersMaps to
GET /v1/snapshotsprogram_id (required), url, profile, open_only, pagingListSnapshots
GET /v1/snapshots/{id}include=body,normalized,features, raw=trueGetSnapshot

Snapshot ids carry the prefix snp_. JSON field names are the proto field names (snake case); optional strings are omitted when empty. A snapshot: id, program_id, entity_id, url, url_template, profile, run_id, fetched_at, last_seen, valid_from, valid_to, status, mime, kind, body_sha256, raw_size, stored_size, normalizer_version, parent_id, lineage_parent_id, outcome, coverage, degraded, pinned, pin_reason, created_at, plus body, body_raw, normalized, headers, features, findings (rule_id, preview, keyed_hash) when included. A change summary: outcome, levels, class, newness, cause, baseline, score, score_features, changes (op, kind, key, newness, value), summary. A diff artifact: doc_id, kind, outcome, levels, algorithm, bound_level, fallback, inserted, deleted, unified, side_by_side, feature_changes. A hit: score, at, diff_id, snapshot_id, from_snapshot_id, url, doc, header, kind, side, feature, mime, url_template, class, newness, cause, vendor, text, decoded_from.

Errors follow 12.2. Content reasons: NOT_FOUND (404), VALIDATION_FAILED (400; query errors carry the textindex code and span of 40.3 in the error metadata), QUOTA_EXCEEDED (429; metadata quota = captures_per_day | stored_bytes | index_bytes, Retry-After for the daily quota), CAPTURE_OUT_OF_ORDER (409), VERSION_CONFLICT (retried internally), CONTENT_INTEGRITY (500: stored bytes failed verification), INTERNAL.

38. Lab and end-to-end acceptance

An in-process lab (services/internal/lab) is a small deterministic internet: an in-scope TLS listener on 127.0.0.1 with virtual hosts and one Ed25519 certificate signed by the lab CA, an in-scope plain-HTTP listener, an out-of-scope listener on 127.0.0.2 (Linux loopback /8), an authoritative DNS server (A records for the lab hosts, NODATA for other types, NXDOMAIN inside the lab zones, REFUSED outside), a clock that jumps to each deploy's time, and logs of every request and DNS question. Volatile values ({{nonce}}, {{ts}}, {{ts_s}}, {{iso}}, {{request_id}}) come from SplitMix64 with the manifest's seed.

A site is conformance/lab/<site>/manifest.json: hosts with scope in or out; deploys (files added, replaced or removed per listener, host and path; redirects); and expect: the planted level-3 changes (deploy, snapshot URL, <op> <kind>|<key>, a regex that finds it), the renamed bundles and their predecessors, notifier rules with alert counts, and robots-disallowed paths. The acceptance site shop has six weekly deploys: d1 baseline; d2 only volatile values, formatting, build hashes and a reordered, renamed bundle; d3 a new POST form, a third-party script without SRI, a leaked key (the AWS documentation example pair), CSP loosened, X-Frame-Options removed, HttpOnly dropped, renamed chunks with new admin endpoints and a library bump; d4 CSP removed, a DELETE endpoint in a renamed bundle and in the OpenAPI document; d5 and d6 only per-response volatile values.

TestLabAcceptance_Memory and TestLabAcceptance_Postgres start the lab and the dev stack (engine, tool runner, egress proxy, scope, content, graph, notifier), create a program allowing the in-scope hosts, register monitor.ff and run it once per deploy. Each item MUST hold:

ItemAssertionMeasured (2026-11-13)
1 capture each deployevery page, script, style and map the deploy serves was requested in that deploy and has a stored version valid at its timeall 6 deploys
2 exactly the planted level-3 changesthe level-3 changes of all stored versions equal the planted ones, each alertable; no text-only (structural) change; each run's report lists exactly its deploy's changed versions18 = 18 over 49 versions
3 renamed chunks linkedeach renamed bundle's first version has its predecessor as lineage parent, and the graph has File → File previous_version5 renames
4 alerts once eachper rule exactly the planted count; the webhook received each once with a valid signature; content alerts carry diff; none carries the key19 deliveries
5 searchableeach planted change found at its version by search_changes, by kind:<kind>_<op> and by the manifest's regex18 × 2 queries
6 no out-of-scope contactno request on the out-of-scope listener or for an out-of-scope Host, no DNS question for an out-of-scope name, no robots-disallowed path, refusals recorded for both out-of-scope hosts254 requests and 538 DNS questions, 0 out of scope
7 storage under 25 % of rawraw snapshots (capture record + redacted body of every version, CSTORE-INV-08's measure) against the versions' raw size6.8 % (all records with diffs 18.4 %)

Run: go test ./services/internal/lab/ (memory) and FF_TEST_PG_DSN=... go test ./services/internal/lab/ -run Postgres.

39. Invariant register

Every v2 package's numbered invariants, each with its test (Test<Pkg>Inv<NN>_<Name>, name-scanned by verify-module through the required ids in modules.toml) and its one-line statement. The Rust port reimplements exactly this list under the same ids (snake case: html_inv_01_token_spans_tile_input). Fuzz targets assert the same invariants on arbitrary input. The list is generated from the package READMEs, which MUST stay in step with this table; every test named here exists.

htmlparse

IdTestStatement
HTML-INV-01TestHTMLInv01_TokenSpansTileInputToken spans start at 0, are contiguous and the EOF token ends at len(input), in every initial state.
HTML-INV-02TestHTMLInv02_ParsingIsTotalTokenize, Parse and ParseFragment accept any bytes without panicking; Parse always yields an html document element.
HTML-INV-03TestHTMLInv03_SerializeReparseFixpointFor round-trip-safe trees (roundTripSafe: well-formed generated documents and the safe subset of soup), Parse(Render(Parse(x))) dumps like Parse(x).
HTML-INV-04TestHTMLInv04_LinearTimeAndAllocationAllocation count and bytes per input byte at 4n are within 20 % of n; time per byte within 50 % (skipped under -race/-short).
HTML-INV-05TestHTMLInv05_ArenaIntegrityAll links are in range and mutually consistent, sibling chains are acyclic, every node is reached exactly once from a parentless node.
HTML-INV-06TestHTMLInv06_DepthLimitThe stack of open elements never exceeds MaxDepth; exceeding it reports the error, keeps every element and keeps parsing.
HTML-INV-07TestHTMLInv07_DecodeTokenizeEquivalenceValid UTF-8 decodes to itself with or without a BOM or utf-8 label, and tokenizes identically.
HTML-INV-08TestHTMLInv08_ErrorsOrderedAndLocatedErrors are sorted by offset, lie within the input, and Line/Col (UTF-16 columns, CR LF = one break) match the offset.
HTML-INV-09TestHTMLInv09_AttributesUniqueAndLimitedNo element has duplicate (namespace, name) attributes; MaxAttributes caps each tag with an error.
HTML-INV-10TestHTMLInv10_TextCoalescedThe parser never leaves two adjacent Text siblings.
HTML-INV-11TestHTMLInv11_NamedReferenceLongestMatchBoth lookup strategies return the brute-force longest entity prefix; entities.json equals the table.

jsparse

IdTestStatement
JS-INV-01TestJSInv01_TokenTilingWith Options.Tokens, tokens and trivia tile the input exactly once, in order, for every valid input and, in tolerant mode, for every input.
JS-INV-02TestJSInv02_Totality, FuzzJSParseTolerant mode returns a program for any byte string and never panics; strict mode returns an error or a program; diagnostics have known codes and in-range spans.
JS-INV-03TestJSInv03_PrintReparse, TestJSInv03_PrintReparseGenerated, FuzzJSPrintReparseParse(Print(p)) has the same canonical JSON as p, in readable and minified layouts (all pass files, generated programs, and the whole positive test262 corpus in TestJSTest262Full).
JS-INV-04TestJSInv04_LinearTimeParsing a 4n-byte bundle costs at most 1.6 × 4 × the time and allocated bytes of an n-byte bundle (best of five).
JS-INV-05TestJSInv05_LexicalGoalsEvery /-initial token is a RegularExpressionLiteral exactly where the grammar admits one (hand-written ambiguous contexts and the generator's recorded answers); regex tokens are lexed under a RegExp goal.
JS-INV-06TestJSInv06_ArenaIntegrityChild ids are in range and not self; list ranges are inside Children; holes only in array elements; no node has two parents; child spans lie within parent spans; in successful parses every node is reachable from the root.
JS-INV-07TestJSInv07_TolerantAgreesOn valid input tolerant mode gives the strict tree and no diagnostics.

JS-INV-09TestJSInv09_CanonicalJSONThe canonical JSON is valid JSON with strictly sorted object keys and is identical across parses.
JS-INV-10TestJSInv10_LexerTiling, FuzzJSLexerThe lexer alone, under any goal sequence, terminates and tiles the input with tokens and trivia.

cssparse

IdTestStatement
CSS-INV-01TestCSSInv01_TokenTilingToken spans are non-empty, contiguous, and cover the input exactly once.
CSS-INV-02TestCSSInv02_TotalityEvery entry point accepts any byte string and yields a well-formed arena (parent/child links, ordered in-range spans); URL extraction never fails.

CSTORE-INV-09TestCStoreInv09_ValidityIntervalsContiguous, disjoint [valid_from, valid_to) per key with one open version; unchanged re-fetches add no row; degraded captures are closed, empty and never open; as-of lookups return the containing version; unseen older captures are refused.
CSTORE-INV-10TestCStoreInv10_SearchDefaultsHunk sides are separate documents; vendored bundles are excluded unless asked; decoded blobs are searchable in decoded only; first appeared returns the earliest adding snapshot, baselines included; query errors are VALIDATION_FAILED.
CSTORE-INV-11TestCStoreInv11_SPAEndToEndRenamed bundles link to their predecessors (lineage key, then SimHash) and an unrelated chunk does not; masked-only versions produce no documents; planted changes are findable by field query and regex; events carry a decodable observation and a valid patch; baselines are not alertable, the planted change is.
CSTORE-INV-12TestCStoreInv12_VolatilityLearnedA non-feature attribute changing on every capture of a stable page is frozen once (version 1), later captures use the learned normalizer and diff masked-only; feature paths are never counted.
CSTORE-INV-13TestCStoreInv13_ObservationReplayThe re-published observation of an unchanged re-fetch (the open version’s stored processed capture and decrypted findings, observed at the re-fetch’s fetch time) equals processing the re-fetched capture afresh, byte for byte.
CSTORE-INV-14TestCStoreInv14_RederiveIdempotent (+Postgres)After a normalizer upgrade, re-deriving in batches of one, in one batch, and with three concurrent jobs reach the same state, every snapshot carries the new stamp, and a second run re-derives nothing and changes nothing; a new stamp with an unchanged pipeline rewrites no record.
CSTORE-INV-15TestCStoreInv15_RederiveNoChange (+Postgres)Re-derive inserts no snapshot, diff, finding or outbox event, allocates no diff seq, closes or touches no version and learns no volatility; ids, sides, validity, lineage and seqs are kept and the new normalizer_version recorded; an ingest after the upgrade re-derives its predecessor first, so its diff is between snapshots of one derivation.
CSTORE-INV-16TestCStoreInv16_RederiveEqualsFreshIngest (+Postgres)After re-derive, every snapshot's normalized text, features record and derived columns, every diff's levels, stats, hunks and change set, and the search and first-appeared answers of a query set equal those of a store that ingested the same captures under the new pipeline; this holds for a normalizer upgrade that changes every page and for one under which a snapshot's own records do not change after a predecessor whose records did (the diff into it is recomputed; TestRegress_RederiveUnchangedSuccessorDiff).

42. Part I errata

Corrections to Part I where its text did not describe the code. Part I keeps its line numbers (docs/spec.txt lines 1 to 4367 are stable), so each correction there is made within the existing line and points here when the full statement needs more room. The code is the reference.

42.1 Bytecode module details (8.3, 8.7, 8.8; G-LANG-06)

The module layout of 8.3 with every field the encoder writes (bytecode/encode.go; all integers little-endian):

magic "FFBC" | format_version u16 = 1 | flags u16
CONSTS  u32 count, then (tag u8, len u32, payload)*
          tag 1 Int, 4 Duration (ns), 5 Time (Unix ns): len 8, i64 two's complement
          tag 2 Float: len 8, IEEE 754 bits, finite only; tag 3 String: UTF-8, at most 1 MiB
TYPES   u32 count, then per entry: kind u8, name u32, and
          record:    nfields u16, (name u32, type u32)*
          enum:      nvariants u16, (name u32, arity u8, type u32 * arity)*
          signature: func u16, nparams u16, (name u32, type u32)*, result u32
          (a type reference is a String constant holding DSL type text; 0xFFFFFFFF is none)
FUNCS   u32 count, then per function: name u32, arity u8, nlocals u16, ncaps u8,
          code_len u32, code, nspans u32, (code_offset u32, source_offset u32, len u32)*
          (spans strictly increasing and inside the code)
EFFECTS u16 mask
TOOLS   u16 count, then (name u32, spec_sha256 [32]byte)*
META    compiler_version (u32 len, bytes), source_sha256 [32]byte,
          program_policy_sha256 [32]byte, workflow_name (u32 len, bytes)
ANNOTS  only when flags has bit 1 (value 2, FlagAnnotations): timeout_ns i64, max_effects u32,
          flags u8 (bit 0 on_unknown_retry, bit 1 idempotent), nsites u32, then per site:
          func u16, code_offset u32, source_offset u32, source_len u32, timeout_ns i64,
          retry_max u32, backoff_initial_ns i64, backoff_max_ns i64, flags u8 (bit 0 idempotent)
entry   u16
  • Flags: bit 1 (FlagAnnotations) is the only flag format version 1 defines; bit 0 is reserved and every other bit MUST be 0. Without the flag the layout is exactly that of 8.3. ANNOTS carries the annotations the engine enforces (workflow #[timeout], #[effects(max)], #[on_unknown(retry)], #[idempotent]; per EFFECT instruction the #[timeout] and #[retry] of the enclosing let statement), keyed by (function, code offset); sites are sorted and unique and each names an EFFECT instruction (verifier rule annotations).
  • Reserved type ids: Option 0, Result 1, Host 2, Severity 3, Tuple 4, ScopeDenied 5, Approval 6, Denied 7, PlanError 8, TaskError 9, LogLevel 10, Budget 11, Plan 12; 13 to 31 are reserved and rejected; user types start at 32 (bytecode/types.go). The VM numbers Option’s variants None = 0, Some = 1 while the DSL declares Some | None; the compiler maps them, and every other enum keeps declaration order.
  • Failure codes (8.7) are numbered 1 to 15 in the order 8.7 lists them; code 16 type_mismatch is added: the verifier checks stack shape, not value types, so a module not produced by a correct compiler can apply an instruction to the wrong kind of value, and the VM fails the run with this code.
  • Stack bound (8.8): the module stores no per-function maximum stack depth. The verifier computes each function’s maximum operand depth and requires nlocals + max_depth ≤ 65,536; the VM checks the total once per call. “Each local assigned once” is per path and per iteration: a LOOP back edge contributes no local definitions, a local written before a loop cannot be written in it, and loop state lives on the stack.
  • Tool calls: EFFECT has no tool operand; the tool name is the last argument, pushed by the CONST immediately before the EFFECT (which must not be a jump target), and the VM moves it to args[0] of the suspension. The effect mask is u16 with bits 8 to 11 for the engine effects scope, approval, alert and plan.
